#### 2026-10-28 · Change password from the app, and read and close the linked devices

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9, SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/password-change-and-device-list. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is part of the plan to "finish M1 by finishing the pending task that we left until sofia's PRs were merged". Seguridad (`CfgSeguridad`) has "Cambiar contraseña" and "Dispositivos activos". This branch is the data under that screen; the screen itself is the next branch of the stack. Claude Code (Opus) planned and reviewed. A Sonnet subagent wrote the code. Claude Code added the same-account check, ran the checks again, and split the work into two PRs to keep each one reviewable.

##### What was done

* **Change password:**
  * The OIDC adapter has `changePassword()`: `authorize` with `kc_action=UPDATE_PASSWORD`, and Keycloak's `kc_action_status` read from `authorizeAdditionalParameters`.
  * `SessionService.changePassword()` returns `changed`, `cancelled` or `failed`. Keycloak always answers with a new session (the browser is ephemeral, so it asks for the login first). The app keeps the new tokens and closes the old session, so a cancelled change doesn't leave two sessions open.
  * **The same account only.** On that login page someone could sign in with another account. The session compares the `sub` of the new and old id tokens (`subjectOf` in `domain/session.ts`). If they differ or can't be read, it ends the new session, keeps the old one, and returns `failed`.
  * A sign out while the browser is open keeps the app signed out. Concurrent calls share one run.
  * The dev session answers `cancelled`.
* **Profile:** `passwordUpdatedAt` is read from `GET /me` when the API sends it.
* **Devices API:**
  * `list` (`GET /devices`);
  * `remove` (`DELETE /devices/{id}`; a 404 counts as done);
  * `signOutOthers` (`POST /devices/sign-out-others`).
* **`useLinkedDevices`:** the list sorted with this phone first, `gcTime: 0`, and the two actions.
  * If this phone was removed (410), the device service signs out and wipes.
  * If it is unknown (404), the device service registers it again.
* **Domain, pure:**
  * `relativeTime` ("hace 3 meses", "ahora" under 2 minutes);
  * `sortDevices`, `otherDevices` and `sessionsLabel` ("1 sesión", "N sesiones").

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (593 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* The Keycloak side of the change is covered by `TestIntegrationAppInitiatedPasswordChange` in #78.
* Not checked on a phone.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Why the account check matters:** without it, signing in as someone else on the change page would put that account's tokens on top of this phone's vault.
* **Tokens are only decoded to compare `sub`.** The API still checks the signature of every token.
* **Splitting the work:** the whole Seguridad change was about 1,640 lines. With GitHub stacks, splitting it into the data and the screen costs nothing.

##### Next step

`feature/linked-devices-screen`: the Seguridad screen on top of this branch.

#### 2026-10-28 · Only an active device can close other sessions

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9. Branch: fix/pending-device-cannot-remove-devices. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While planning M2, a security gap turned up in M1. Daniel approved fixing it as part of the M2 plan, then said "continue".

##### What was done

* **The gap:** `DELETE /devices/{id}` and `POST /devices/sign-out-others` didn't check the calling device. Someone who only knows the password could log in on a new phone, which starts `pending`, and remove the real phone. That phone then wipes its vault on its next start.
* **`devices/service.go`:**
  * `Remove` takes the caller's device id. A device may always remove itself, even when pending, because that is signing out.
  * Removing another device, or signing out the others, needs an active caller. A pending, removed, unknown or missing caller gets `ErrNotActive`.
  * The check runs in the same transaction and advisory lock as the removal.
  * The caller is checked first, so a pending phone can't probe which device ids exist.
* **`devices_http.go`:** `ErrNotActive` answers 403 `device_not_active`.
  * `DELETE` takes the caller from the verified `X-Device-Id`.
  * `sign-out-others` keeps the raw header, so a removed phone still gets 410 and wipes itself.
* **`identity.yaml`:** the 403 on both operations.
* **`ports/ports.go`, for M2:** the `Devices` port.
  * `Get` returns a device's name, platform, public key and status.
  * `RemoveOthers` is for the backup reset. It removes the other devices, revokes their Keycloak sessions and audits each one.
  * `NewSessionRevoker` reuses the Keycloak admin client.
* **Tests:**
  * service tests: pending and active callers, self-removal, blank, unknown and removed callers;
  * HTTP tests for the 403;
  * port tests;
  * the Postgres HTTP integration test: a pending phone can't remove the active one or sign out the others, a request without a device can't remove one, and a pending phone can remove itself.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passes (0 issues), and so does `make test-go`.
* The integration tests pass on a throwaway Postgres 16.15: identity, migrate, and the Keycloak testcontainers tests.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The app is not changed here.** A 403 shows Seguridad's generic "couldn't do it" alert, with no wipe. M2's identity work hides "Cerrar" on a pending phone.
* **Revocation inside the port is best effort,** like the HTTP path. Its error is dropped because it may carry ids.

##### Next step

M2's backup reset and device approvals use the `Devices` port.

#### 2026-10-28 · GET /me and PATCH /me

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/profile-endpoints. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same plan as the admin client. `docs/modules/M01-identity.md` asks for `GET /me` (masked email, name, verified flag, creation date, cached 5 minutes) and `PATCH /me` (first and last name, written to Keycloak). The plan adds `passwordUpdatedAt` for the design's "Actualizada hace 3 meses" in `CfgSeguridad`.

##### What was done

* **`contracts/modules/identity.yaml`:** `getProfile` and `updateProfile`, with the schemas `IdentityProfile` and `IdentityProfileUpdate` and fictional examples. Problem codes:
  * `invalid_profile` (400);
  * `profile_not_found` (404);
  * `identity_admin_unavailable` (503).
* **`mask.go`:** the email masking rule, `camila@correo.com` to `ca•••@correo.com`. It counts characters, not bytes. Input that isn't an address gives only the dots.
* **`profile_http.go`:**
  * `GET /me` reads the user and the password date through the admin client and caches the response in Redis for 5 minutes under `identity:me:<sub>`. The cache holds the masked email only.
  * `PATCH /me` validates both names, writes them to Keycloak and clears the cache. A valid name is trimmed, has 1 to 50 characters, contains only letters of any script, marks, spaces, apostrophes, hyphens and periods, and starts with a letter.
  * Without Redis, or when Redis fails, the request goes on without the cache.
* **`module.go`:** builds the admin client from the config. `/me` is no longer a 501 stub.
* **Tests:** handlers over a fake admin and an in-memory Redis hook. They cover:
  * the mask;
  * empty names;
  * the cache and its TTL;
  * a Redis failure;
  * validation in both scripts;
  * errors without values;
  * 404 and 503.

##### Result

* `make generate`, `go vet` with both tags, `go test ./...`, `make lint-go` and `make lint-mobile` are clean.
* `make test-integration` and `make test-keycloak` passed on the full D2 work before the split. Claude Code ran them again after the split.

##### Decisions

* **An account that is missing from Keycloak gives 404 `profile_not_found`.** The token is still valid, so 401 doesn't fit, and the problem isn't temporary, so 503 doesn't either. The app can sign out when it sees it.
* **The names stay only in Keycloak**, as M01 says. CLAUDE.md's "Where data lives" lists the profile name under the `identity` schema; the docs PR aligns that row.

##### Problems

* The generated server doesn't enforce the contract's lengths, so the handler checks them itself.

##### Next step

* `feature/revoke-device-sessions`: removing a device ends its Keycloak session.

#### 2026-10-28 · Take Keycloak's access_denied as a cancel, not a failure

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9. Branch: fix/reset-return-is-not-an-error. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

It came out of the theme-in-image work of the approved M1 plan. After a password reset, the Keycloak theme sends the app back with `error=access_denied`, and the app has to take that as "back to Inicio". Claude Code (Opus) planned and reviewed. A Sonnet subagent wrote the fix, and Claude Code ran the checks again itself.

##### What was done

* **`adapters/oidc/appAuthOidc.ts`:** `access_denied`, as a whole word, joins the patterns that mean `cancelled`.
* **`appAuthOidc.test.ts`:**
  * `classifyAppAuthError` table rows: `access_denied` is a cancel; `not_access_denied_at_all` and `unauthorized_client` stay `rejected`;
  * a new `authorize` test: an `access_denied` rejection ends as `cancelled`.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile` passes, and the identity tests pass (6 suites, 56 tests). Prettier is clean.
* Pull request: see the branch.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Where `access_denied` comes from.** In react-native-app-auth 9.0.0 it reaches JavaScript as the error `code`:
  * iOS: `RNAppAuth.m`, `getErrorCode`, maps `OIDErrorCodeOAuthAuthorizationAccessDenied` to `access_denied`;
  * Android: `RNAppAuthModule.java` uses `ex.error`, the redirect's `error` parameter.
  * The message isn't relied on. The AppAuth sources themselves aren't in `node_modules`, so the Android value is inferred from AppAuth's documented behavior.

#### 2026-10-28 · Give the vault what it needs from the session and the device key

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/identity-session-for-vault. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is part of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"). The vault needs several things from identity: a fresh login to unlock without biometrics, the time of the last login, this phone's status, and an X25519 key agreement with the device key for the device approval. It must get them without ever seeing a token or the private key.

##### What was done

* **`domain/session.ts`:** `authTimeOf(jwt)` reads the id token's `auth_time`. `subjectOf` and `authTimeOf` now share one decoder.
* **`SessionService`:**
  * `lastLoginAt()` returns the time of the last typed login. Keycloak keeps `auth_time` across refreshes.
  * `reauthenticate()` runs `authorize` with `prompt=login` and `max_age=0`, and reuses `changePassword`'s same-account guard, now one `adopt()` helper. It returns `ok` only if the new `auth_time` is no older than the moment the call started, minus 60 s for clock skew. A Keycloak session that skipped the login form can't unlock the vault.
  * A sign-out during the browser step keeps the app signed out, and concurrent calls share one run.
* **Device:**
  * `currentDevice()` returns the id and status from `GET /devices/current`, handling 410 like `sync()`.
  * `devicePublicKey()` returns this phone's public key.
  * `deviceSharedSecret(peer)` does the X25519 inside identity, so the private key never leaves it.
* **`index.ts`:** exports these, plus `onSessionChange` for code outside React.
* **Seguridad:** on a pending phone, "Cerrar" and "Cerrar todas las demás sesiones" are hidden and "Este dispositivo espera aprobación" is shown. The API now refuses those calls from a pending phone.
* **Tests:**
  * `authTimeOf`;
  * `lastLoginAt` across sign in, refresh and sign out;
  * `reauthenticate`: ok, cancelled, failed, another account, a stale or missing `auth_time`, sign-out during the browser step, concurrent calls;
  * `currentDevice`;
  * the RFC 7748 §6.1 vectors for the shared secret;
  * the exports;
  * the pending phone's Seguridad, in light and dark.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The fresh-login check was added during review.** Without it, `ok` would only mean "the browser came back with the same account".
* **The dev session** answers `ok` with `lastLoginAt` set to now. It has no device, so the device functions return null or reject.

##### Next step

The vault's lock screen and biometrics call `reauthenticate()` and `lastLoginAt()`. The device approval uses `deviceSharedSecret()`.

#### 2026-10-28 · Write the backup module's API contract

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62, SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/backup-contract. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel pasted the M2 prompt: "You are building module M2 of Fynk: the encrypted vault on the phone, biometric unlock, the zero knowledge backup of manual data, and moving that data to a new phone." He approved the plan and said "continue". This contract is the plan's first pull request, so the backend and the app can both build on it.

##### What was done

* **`contracts/modules/backup.yaml`:** the nine endpoints of the shared contracts §5, plus two new ones decided in planning. M0's health example is removed. The contract covers:
  * the encrypted records: at most 200 per call, an atomic batch, `stale_version`, a 5 MB quota, and a cursor page;
  * the recovery key: PUT only from an active device. GET never returns the proof hash;
  * `POST /backup/recovery-key/activate`: a pending phone proves it opened the wrapped key, with no decryptable answer and 5 tries per hour;
  * `POST /backup/reset`: only after a password reset by email code in the last 10 minutes;
  * the device approvals: create, the pending list for the old phone, the state for the new phone, approve and reject.

  Every operation needs `X-Device-Id`, and every response has an example for Prism.
* **`internal/backup/module.go`:** the generated server isn't mounted yet, so every route answers 501 through `module.Stub`. The implementation pull requests mount it.
* **`internal/app/app_test.go`:** skips backup in the health path test, as it already does for identity.
* **`00-shared-contracts.md`:** the two endpoints in §5, and `backup.reset` in the audit event list (§4.3).
* **`M02-vault.md`:** one line each for the two endpoints.

##### Result

* `make generate` and `make lint-go` pass (0 issues), as do `make test-go` and `make format-check`.
* Prism serves the new routes with their examples.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Activation by proof (decided with Daniel):** a phone that restores with the code becomes active only if SHA-256 of `HKDF(DEK, 'fynk/device-proof/v1')` matches the hash saved with the recovery key. Otherwise anyone with the password could activate a phone and overwrite the backup and the recovery key.
* **Reset after an email-code password reset (decided with Daniel):** "No tengo ninguno de los dos" deletes the old backup. Requiring the email code means a password alone cannot destroy someone's backup.
* **The approval state enum is its own schema** (`BackupApprovalState`). The inline enum gave oapi-codegen a duplicate type name.
* **No handler is mounted yet,** so no hand-written 501 responses are needed.

##### Next step

The backend stack implements the endpoints, and the app builds on the generated types.

#### 2026-10-28 · Prove the server stores only ciphertext

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: test/backup-records-integration. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The M2 prompt's test: "A stored record never contains the plaintext bytes of the test payload". It runs through the real HTTP stack, identity and Postgres.

##### What was done

* **`postgres/records_integration_test.go`:**
  * a known plaintext is sealed with AES-256-GCM and uploaded. The raw row text and the bytea columns are searched for it, and it is never there. The API round trip decrypts back to the original.
  * a real pending device gets 403 on GET and PUT, and an unknown device gets 403 on GET;
  * paging with `limit=2` returns every record once in seq order, and a record updated after page 1 comes back at the end;
  * a stale version gets 409, an equal-version retry is accepted, and a duplicate in one batch gets 400;
  * against real rows, 79 full-size records fit the 5 MB quota and the 80th gets 413 with nothing stored. A smaller new version then lets the batch fit.

#### 2026-10-28 · Store the encrypted backup records

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-records-store. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the next step of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): "PUT /backup/records … 409 stale_version when the version is lower, a 5 MB per-user quota counted with octet_length in the transaction". This PR is the storage half. The HTTP endpoints are the next PR of the stack.

##### What was done

* **Migration 2:** a `seq` column from `backup.records_seq`, indexed by user. It is the paging cursor; timestamps are not safe across API instances.
* **`records/`:**
  * the envelope, the limits and the opaque cursor (base64url of the seq, decoded strictly);
  * the service: only an active device may read or write, a batch is stored all or nothing, and `backup.uploaded` is audited with the count only.
* **`postgres/records.go`:**
  * a per-user advisory lock serializes writes and page reads, so seq order is commit order and a page never skips a row committed later;
  * the version check is in the upsert itself (`WHERE version <= EXCLUDED.version`), so a lower version stores nothing and becomes `stale_version`;
  * the quota sums ciphertext plus nonce bytes in the same transaction.
* **Tests:** the service against fakes (pending, removed and unknown devices, stale, quota, cursors, audit meta), and the migration version check.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass for the backup module, alone on this commit.
* The Postgres integration tests pass with `-p 1` on Postgres 16.15.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The implementer stopped on the weekly usage limit** and was resumed after it reset. Its work was split into three PRs to stay near 400 lines: this one is 527 lines, 206 of them tests.

##### Next step

The HTTP endpoints (`feature/backup-records-endpoints`).

#### 2026-10-28 · Backup schema and the recovery key rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-recovery-key-store. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the next step of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): the backend schema and the rules for the recovery key, including activating a new phone with proof of the key.

##### What was done

* **`backup/migrations`:**
  * the three tables of the M2 prompt: `records`, `recovery_keys` and `device_approvals`;
  * `recovery_keys.device_proof_hash`, the SHA-256 of `HKDF(DEK, 'fynk/device-proof/v1')`, never returned;
  * CHECK constraints on the key sizes (48, 12, 16 and 32 bytes);
  * indexes for paging records and for finding approvals.
* **`backup/recovery`:**
  * **`Set`:** only an active device; it replaces the old key, so the old code stops working, and audits `recovery_key.set`.
  * **`Get`:** never returns the hash.
  * **`Activate`:** compares `sha256(proof)` with the stored hash in constant time. On a match it activates the device through identity's `Activator` and audits `device.approved` and `backup.restored` with `method=recovery_code`.
  * **The failure limit:** 5 failed proofs per user per hour. Redis counts them when it's configured, otherwise an in-process rolling window does.
* **`backup/postgres`:** the repository, an upsert.
* **`module.go`:** the schema is migrated. Every route still answers 501 until the endpoint PR mounts them.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passes (0 issues), and so does `make test-go`.
* The Postgres integration tests pass on a throwaway Postgres 16.15. They cover replacing a key, isolation per user and the size check.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Split from the endpoints** to keep each pull request under 400 lines. The handlers and the HTTP integration test come in the next PR of the stack.
* **The activation limit is checked before the comparison and counted only on a mismatch.** Two parallel guesses could slip past the count by one. That is acceptable because the proof is 256 bits derived from a 128-bit code: the limit is a second line of defense, not the main one.
* **A failed audit write after activation answers 500.** The device stays active, and a retry is a no-op. This is the same order identity uses.

##### Next step

The endpoint PR mounts `PUT` and `GET /backup/recovery-key` and the activate route.

##### What was done

* **`adapters/sqlite/opSqliteDriver.ts`:** op-sqlite with SQLCipher.
  * op-sqlite passes `encryptionKey` straight to `sqlite3_key_v2` (read in `OPBridge.cpp`). The DEK goes as SQLCipher's raw key `x'<64 hex>'`, so there is no passphrase derivation on top.
  * op-sqlite is loaded lazily, so importing `@modules/vault` touches no native code.
* **`application/vault.ts`, the engine:**
  * **Refuses to open when `PRAGMA cipher_version` is empty.** A build without SQLCipher must never write a plain file. It then reads `sqlite_master`, so a wrong key fails at once.
  * **Runs each module's migrations** in registration order, tracked in `vault_migrations(module, version, applied_at)`. It checks the `<module>_` table prefix and the versions. A module that registers after unlock gets its tables right away.
  * **One queue for everything:** transactions, migrations, and statements outside a transaction. A statement never lands inside another module's open transaction, where it would be rolled back with it.
  * **A kept `VaultDb` throws `VaultLockedError`** once the vault is closed.
  * **`wipe()`** closes, deletes the file and always removes both copies of the key, even if the delete fails. Without its key, a left-over file can never be read. It then emits `vault.locked`.
* **`adapters/keychain/dekStore.ts`:**
  * copy A in `co.fynk.app.vault.key`, `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`, no cloud sync;
  * `co.fynk.app.vault.key.biometric` is reserved for copy B;
  * a corrupt entry reads as no key.
* **`index.ts`:** the real `registerVaultMigrations`, `getVaultDb` and `wipe`. M1 already calls `wipe` on a removed phone; with no vault yet it just clears the keychain.
* **Tests:**
  * the engine on a real SQLite (sql.js), plus a fake for the SQLCipher check;
  * the op-sqlite open options and pragmas, with a mock;
  * the keychain options;
  * random DEK creation.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (50 suites, 682 tests) and `make format-check` pass.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Three fixes made during review:**
  * statements outside a transaction now wait in the queue;
  * a kept `VaultDb` checks the lock;
  * `wipe` removes the keys in a `finally`.
* **"The file cannot be read without the key" can only be shown on a phone.** SQLCipher is native and Jest runs on Node. On a phone: open `fynk-vault.db` with `sqlite3` and expect "file is not a database". The `cipher_version` guard stops a build without SQLCipher from writing anything.
* **Nothing opens the vault yet.** The lock screen PR wires it to the session.

Lock states, AppState and the lock screen.

#### 2026-10-28 · The vault's key hierarchy as pure functions

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-8, SCRUM-62, SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/vault-key-crypto. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the first code of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"). The prompt's key hierarchy becomes pure, tested functions: "BK backup key = HKDF-SHA256(DEK, info 'fynk/backup/v1')… Recovery code: 128 random bits shown as 26 Crockford base32 characters in 5 groups… 6 digit approval code: the first 4 bytes of SHA-256 of the new device public key…".

##### What was done

* **`domain/crypto.ts`:**
  * `seal` and `open`: AES-256-GCM through `@noble/ciphers`, with AAD. Any change to the ciphertext, tag, nonce, key or AAD throws `VaultCryptoError`, which says nothing about the data.
  * `deriveBackupKey` and `deriveDeviceProof`: HKDF-SHA256 with `fynk/backup/v1` and `fynk/device-proof/v1`.
  * `deviceProofHash`.
  * `recordAad`: `userId|collection|recordId|version`.
  * `approvalCode`: the 6-digit code from the new phone's public key.

  No randomness inside: keys and nonces are parameters.
* **`domain/recoveryCode.ts`:**
  * `encodeRecoveryCode` (16 bytes to 26 characters) and `formatRecoveryCode` (5, 5, 5, 5 and 6);
  * `parseRecoveryCode`: accepts spaces, hyphens and lowercase, maps I and L to 1 and O to 0, and rejects U, other characters, a wrong length and non-zero padding;
  * `decodeRecoveryCode`.
* **Tests, all with fixed vectors:**
  * GCM checked against Node's `createCipheriv` and NIST GCM test case 16, and tampering with every part fails;
  * HKDF checked against Node's `hkdfSync`;
  * the approval code of 32 zero bytes is `123181`;
  * the recovery code of `00 01 … 0F` is `000G40R40M30E209185GR38E1W`, checked independently with Python;
  * round trips, tolerant parsing and rejections.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass on the vault branch above this one (50 suites, 682 tests).

##### Decisions and problems

* **Split from the vault engine** to keep each pull request under 400 lines. The engine is the next PR of the stack.
* **The proof's comment says the server sees it once.** A new phone sends it to be activated, and it is one-way: the DEK can't be derived from it.

The SQLCipher engine (`feature/sqlcipher-vault`).

##### Instruction

Step 2 of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): "lock states locked, unlocking, unlocked; AppState: lock after 5 minutes in the background, and on sign out … unlock with copy A when lastLoginAt() is under 5 minutes old; otherwise 'Ingresar con contraseña' calls reauthenticate(); the setup decision of decision 4".

##### What was done

* **`domain/lockPolicy.ts`:** `shouldLock` (more than 5 minutes in the background; exactly 5 doesn't lock) and `isFreshLogin` (a login of the last 5 minutes, tolerating a phone clock up to 5 minutes behind).
* **`application/lockController.ts`:**
  * the states `locked`, `unlocking`, `unlocked`, `pendingDevice` and `error`;
  * **with copy A,** it opens only after a fresh login; otherwise it stays locked until `reauthenticate()` answers `ok`;
  * **without a key,** a pending phone waits for approval, and only an **active** phone gets a new DEK and copy A;
  * it locks on sign out and after more than 5 minutes in the background. A lock during an open closes the vault again;
  * a build without SQLCipher shows `notEncrypted` and is never retried.
* **Identity (M1, SCRUM-6):** `currentDevice()` waits for this sign in's registration before reading. The dev session's phone counts as active.
* **Tests:** the policy, and every state change with fakes and a fixed clock.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (58 suites, 803 tests on the top branch) and `make format-check` pass. This commit passes alone too.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The implementer stopped on the weekly usage limit** and was resumed after it reset.
* **A bug found in review:** `currentDevice()` is `null` while the phone is still registering, when the API doesn't know it, or right after it was removed. The controller treated `null` as the first phone and made a new DEK: a second phone could get its own key before the API had marked it pending, and a removed phone got a key right after its wipe. Now only `active` makes a key, `null` is an error with a retry, and identity waits for the registration first.
* **A reinstall on an active phone with a recovery key on the server** still counts as the first phone here. The recovery code PR asks the server first. Nothing uploads to the backup before then.
* Split into three PRs (controller, screen, gate) to stay near 400 lines. This one is 632 lines, 444 of them tests.

#### 2026-10-29 · The backup reset rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-reset-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Decision 2 of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("Email code reset"): "No tengo ninguno de los dos" needs a password reset by email code in the last 10 minutes. `POST /backup/reset` then deletes the backup and the recovery key, removes every other device and revokes its sessions, activates this phone, and audits `backup.reset`.

##### What was done

* **`reset/service.go`:**
  * the caller's phone may be pending or active, but not removed or unknown;
  * the email-code reset must be at most 10 minutes old, and not more than 1 minute in the future;
  * the caller's Keycloak session must have started after the reset, with 60 seconds of skew;
  * then, in order: the backup is deleted, the other devices are removed and their sessions revoked, a pending caller is activated, and `backup.reset` is audited with `method=email_code`. Every step is idempotent, so a retry within the window finishes the job.
* **`postgres/reset.go`:** one transaction takes the records lock and the approvals lock, then deletes the user's records, recovery key and approvals.
* **`backup.reset`** is defined in the backup module. `internal/audit` belongs to M13 and wasn't touched; the constant can move there later.
* **Tests:** every refusal, including the exact window and skew edges, both callers, and a failure at each step.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass on this commit alone.

The endpoint (`feature/backup-reset`).

#### 2026-10-29 · The device approval rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/device-approval-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Step 3 of the backend plan: "approve calls ActivateDevice; audit device.approved and device.rejected; tests for 'expired cannot approve' and 'not active cannot approve'".

##### What was done

* **Create:** only a pending phone asks. An active phone gets `ErrAlreadyActive`, and a removed or unknown one `ErrNotActive`. The public key comes from identity, never from the request. A new request expires the phone's older open ones.
* **Pending list:** only for an active phone, newest first, skipping requests whose phone was removed or is no longer pending.
* **Get:** only the phone that asked can read its request; any other caller gets not found. A request past its 10 minutes reads as expired. **The wrapped DEK is returned once:** the same transaction clears it and audits `backup.restored` with `method=device_approval`, so a failed audit keeps the key for a retry.
* **Approve and reject:** only an active phone. Not found, not pending, or expired (410) are checked under a row lock, and the new phone must still be pending. Approving stores the grant and activates the new phone before the commit, so a failed activation leaves the request pending. `device.approved` and `device.rejected` carry the method and the deciding device, never key material.
* **Tests:** every rule with fakes and a fixed clock.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass on this commit alone.

##### Decisions and problems

* **An expired request is stored as expired, and then 410 is returned.** The status is committed first, because an error inside the transaction would roll it back.
* **Known gap:** activation runs on identity's own connection. If the approval's commit fails after it, the phone is active and the request stays pending. That phone can still restore with its recovery code.
* **An approved grant never read** stays until the new phone reads it. Only that phone's private key can open it.
* This PR is 789 lines, 522 of them tests.

##### Next step

The endpoints (`feature/device-approval-endpoints`).

#### 2026-10-30 · Approve a new device from the old phone

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/approve-new-device. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the old phone's side of HU-046 on top of `feature/backup-sync`: the approval crypto fixed by ADR 0002, the `/device-approvals` API adapter, the service that seals the DEK for the new phone, an owner check with biometrics or the password, the "Aprobar nuevo dispositivo" screen, an in-app notice, and an "Aprobar" action in M1's Seguridad. The new phone's side belongs to the restore branch.

##### What was done

* **`domain/deviceApproval.ts`:** `approvalKey` (HKDF-SHA256 of the X25519 shared secret, info `fynk/device-approval/v1`), `sealGrant` and `openGrant` with the lowercase approval id as AAD. Tests use vectors made with Node's crypto and a round trip with real X25519 on both sides.
* **`adapters/api/deviceApprovalsApi.ts`:** the five operations with base64 only in the adapter; errors become `ApprovalError(kind)` in `application/ports.ts`.
* **`application/approveDevice.ts`:** `listPending` (adds the 6 digit code), `approve` (owner check, ephemeral key, shared secret, seal, send, wipe), `reject`. X25519 sits behind a `KeyAgreementPort` (`adapters/crypto/x25519KeyAgreement.ts`).
* **`adapters/confirmOwner.ts`:** copy B behind the prompt when it exists, else a fresh Keycloak login and copy A. It always asks, even with the vault open.
* **`ui/ApproveDeviceScreen.tsx`:** polls every 5 s only while focused; card per request with device, platform, code 3+3, minutes left, Aprobar and Rechazar, and every message. Registered as `vault.ApproveDevice`, path `boveda/aprobar`.
* **`application/approvalNotifier.ts`:** on `vault.unlocked` and on foreground (once a minute) it notifies each request through M12, lazily loaded.
* **Identity:** `DeviceRow` and `SecurityScreen` show "Aprobar" on a pending row when this phone is active and navigate to `vault.ApproveDevice` by name.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (122 suites, 1538 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Only a phone can check: the biometric prompt and the Keycloak login of the owner check, and the look of the new screen and the extra button on the device row.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No `isEnabled` check in the notifier:** M12 keeps `device_approval` always on, so a security notice never depends on a switch.
* **A failed password check throws and the service answers `failed`;** only a cancel answers `cancelled`.
* **An invalidated copy B falls back to the password** without deleting copy B; the lock screen does that on its next use.
* **A request whose public key is not 32 bytes is hidden** by the adapter, since it could not be approved.
* **`notFound` is reported as `notPending`:** for the person, the request is gone.
* **The Seguridad route is the literal `identity.Security`** in the vault index, because identity is still loading when the vault index loads (import cycle).
* The new screen and the "Aprobar" button on the device row are not in the design: they need design review.

##### Next step

The restore branch: the new phone creates the request, shows the code, polls and opens the grant.

#### 2026-10-30 · The backup record envelope and registry

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-registry. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel asked to "continue the implementation of M2 and complete what is left". This is the first half of the backup engine on the phone: seal each record, know which collections take part and which records changed, and talk to `PUT` and `GET /backup/records`.

##### What was done

* **`domain/backupEnvelope.ts`:**
  * `backupRecordId`: a UUID stays, and any other id maps to a stable version 8 UUID from SHA-256 of `collection|id`;
  * `sealRecord` and `openRecord`: AES-256-GCM with the record AAD, the plaintext `{id, data}`, and a typed error over 64 KiB.
* **`domain/base64.ts`:** standard base64 with padding, a copy of identity's helpers.
* **`adapters/vault/migrations.ts`:** the table `vault_backup_dirty`, registered in `defaultVault.ts`.
* **`application/backupRegistry.ts`:**
  * `registerBackupCollection` and `markDirty`, with the same rules as the in-memory registry of `@shared/testing`;
  * marks made while locked wait in memory and are written on unlock;
  * an upload clears only the marks it saw.
* **`adapters/api/backupRecordsApi.ts`:** the records API, with base64 fields, `X-Device-Id` and `BackupError` kinds.
* **M1:** `userId()` exported from `@modules/identity` (the `sub` of the session), for the record AAD.
* **Tests:**
  * envelope vectors and tamper cases (ciphertext, nonce, user, collection, id, version, key);
  * a check that the ciphertext never contains the plaintext;
  * the registry rules, the dirty table, and the API with a fake fetch.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.
* Nothing uploads yet. The queue, restore and public exports come in `feature/backup-sync`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The `deleted` flag is not in the AAD,** so `openRecord` also checks that the content agrees with it. A server that flips the flag cannot delete or resurrect a record.
* **`markDirty` is synchronous** and only queues the write, so a module may call it inside its own vault transaction without waiting on it.
* The work was done by a Sonnet 5.5 agent and reviewed by the orchestrating session, which split it from the upload and restore half to keep each pull request reviewable.

##### Next step

The upload queue and restore (`feature/backup-sync`).

#### 2026-10-30 · Upload and restore the encrypted backup

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-sync. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel asked to "continue the implementation of M2 and complete what is left". This is the second half of the backup engine on the phone. It uploads the changed records while the app is open and restores everything on a new phone, against the finished `PUT` and `GET /backup/records`.

##### What was done

* **`application/backupSync.ts`:**
  * the upload runs 5 s after the last change, and at once when the app leaves the foreground;
  * it sends batches of 200, one upload at a time, and clears only the marks it saw;
  * a 409 downloads the newer versions, imports them and retries once;
  * `restoreAll` downloads every page and keeps the highest version of each record;
  * `exportAll` is for M13's data export.
* **`adapters/appStateBackup.ts`:** the AppState watcher for the background flush.
* **`adapters/defaultBackup.ts`:** the default registry and sync, reading copy A for the backup key.
* **`index.ts`:**
  * exports `registerBackupCollection`, `markDirty` and `exportAll`;
  * `wipe()` clears the backup state;
  * `restoreAll` stays inside the module for the restore screens.
* **Tests (fake timers and a sql.js vault):**
  * the debounce, the background flush, 201 records split 200 + 1, and a mark during an upload;
  * 409, 403 and offline;
  * restore paging (a full last page, then an empty one), the highest version, and unknown collections;
  * marks made while locked.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (116 suites, 1447 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The key cache lives in the sync,** and is dropped on `vault.locked` and on wipe, so it never outlives an unlocked vault.
* **A record the API would refuse (over 64 KiB) stays dirty** and does not block the rest of the batch.
* **The 409 retry works because the server refuses only a lower version than the stored one.** After the import, the local version is at least the remote one.
* **`importAll` is called only for collections that have records** in the download.
* **New error kind `noKey`** for `restoreAll` when the key or the account is missing.
* **A server that repeats its cursor** stops the restore with an error instead of looping.
* **The dev session has no registered device,** so the backup stays off there.
* **Only a phone can check** the dirty table upsert on SQLCipher, the AppState flush, and the real quota and cursor.
* The work was done by a Sonnet 5.5 agent and reviewed by the orchestrating session, which split the branch in two to keep each pull request reviewable.

The restore screens on a new phone (`feature/restore-with-recovery-code`), which call `restoreAll` after the phone gets the DEK.

#### 2026-10-30 · The recovery code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62, SCRUM-5. Branch: feature/recovery-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build HU-045 on the finished backend (`PUT` and `GET /backup/recovery-key`): make the recovery code on the first phone, wrap the DEK with a key from the code, show the code once and ask for its last group before uploading. Also "Generar un código nuevo" in Seguridad, and the M1 criterion of SCRUM-5, "Al terminar el registro se genera el código de recuperación".

##### What was done

* **Domain:** `recoveryKey.ts` (`wrapDek`, `unwrapDek` with the AAD `fynk/recovery/v1`, the contract's Argon2 parameters), `matchesRecoveryGroup` in `recoveryCode.ts`, and `base64.ts` for the API fields.
* **Ports and adapters:** `RecoveryKdf`, `RecoveryKeyApi`, `RandomBytes`, `RecoveryError`; `argon2Kdf` over `react-native-argon2` and `recoveryKeyApi` over the shared client.
* **Service:** `createRecoveryCodeService`. `prepare(dek)` makes the code, derives the key and wraps the DEK, and `commit()` is the only upload. `confirmRecoveryCode` compares the typed last group and then commits.
* **Lock controller:** new state `recoveryCode`. The first phone asks the server first: a recovery key means a backup exists, so it makes no key (`pendingDevice`), and a failed question is `offline`. After any open with a key, an active device asks the server and shows a new code when there is none. A network error leaves the vault unlocked.
* **UI:** `RecoveryCodeScreen` (two steps), shown by `VaultFlow` and by the pushed `NewRecoveryCodeScreen`; `RecoveryCodeRow` under "Desbloqueo" in Seguridad. The Maestro signup flow now saves the code.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (119 suites, 1445 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Not run: the Maestro flow, which needs an emulator and the local Keycloak.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The check after an open does not block it.** The vault shows as unlocked at once and the server check runs afterwards, so an offline phone opens normally. When the answer is "no recovery key", the flow replaces the tabs. This can flash the tabs for a moment.
* **The password step of "Generar un código nuevo" runs when the screen opens**, not on the row's tap, so the `boveda/recuperacion` deep link cannot skip it. `UnlockSetting` asks inside the controller on the action; this one asks inside `prepareNewRecoveryCode`.
* **`react-native-argon2` is loaded on first use** (`require` inside `argon2Kdf`). Its native module is read while the library loads, which broke the identity tests that import the vault index.
* The shared `fonts` token has no "mono" family; the code uses `fonts.amount.semibold` (IBM Plex Mono).
* **The dev session has no backup.** With `EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_AUTH` there is no registered device and no API, so asking the server for the recovery key failed and the vault never opened (dev builds and `make e2e`). Identity now exports `backupEnabled()` (false in the dev session) and the lock controller takes it as a dep: when it is false the first phone opens as before with no recovery code, the check after an open returns at once, and "Generar un código nuevo" fails.
* The Argon2 password is the canonical 26 character code. The parameters are the contract's and were not changed.

##### Next step

The restore on a new phone with the recovery code, and the backup records sync.

#### 2026-10-30 · Restore on a new phone with the old phone's approval

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/restore-with-approval. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the new phone's side of HU-046: "Aprobar desde mi otro teléfono". The new phone asks, shows a 6 digit code, waits for the old phone to approve, opens the grant and restores like the code path does. Share the steps after the DEK is known between the two paths.

##### What was done

* **`completeRestore.ts`:** the part of `restoreWithCode` that starts once the DEK is known (wipe, copy A, open, mark, `restoreAll`, clear the mark, undo on failure). Both paths use it; the `restoreWithCode` tests did not change.
* **`restoreWithApproval.ts`:** `start()` makes a random Idempotency-Key, creates the request and returns the id, the code and the expiry (`device_already_active` becomes `alreadyActive`). `check(id)` is one poll: `waiting`, `rejected`, `expired`, `offline`, or, when approved with a grant, shared secret, `openGrant` with the approval id as AAD, `completeRestore`, and the secrets zeroed. Approved without a grant is `grantLost`.
* **`lockController.ts`:** `pendingDevice` carries `deviceStatus` (`pending` or `active`). `startApproval()` and `checkApproval(id)` set `restoring` while the restore runs and `unlocked` at the end. After `failed` or `grantLost` the phone is `active`.
* **UI:** the option card, `ApprovalWaiting` (instruction, code 3+3, "Esperando aprobación…", "Cancelar") and the `useApprovalRestore` hook that polls every 5 s and stops on cancel, a final answer, the expiry and unmount. Messages for rejected, expired, grantLost, offline and failed, with "Pedir de nuevo" for the first two.
* **Wiring:** `defaultLock.ts` passes the approvals API, `devicePublicKey` and `deviceSharedSecret` from identity.
* **Tests:** every outcome of the service with fakes, a full round trip with the real old phone service and an X25519 ephemeral key, the controller states, the screen in light and dark with fake timers.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (134 suites, 1769 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.
* It needs a real pair of phones to see the whole flow.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Option hidden for `active`:** the server refuses an approval for an active phone, so only the code is offered. A 409 at start makes the controller switch to `active` too.
* **`onRestoring` fires when the grant arrives**, not when the restore starts: the server clears the grant on that read, so any failure after it leaves an active phone with no key.
* **Offline during a poll** keeps waiting and shows a small notice.
* **Cancel** only leaves the screen; the request stays open on the server until it expires (10 minutes).
* **Only a phone can check:** the real X25519 key in identity, the old phone showing the same code, and the timing of the poll.

"No tengo ninguno de los dos" (`feature/restore-without-code`).

#### 2026-10-30 · Restore on a new phone with the recovery code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/restore-with-recovery-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

HU-045: "Restore on a new phone with the recovery code". The phone that is pending, or active with no local key while the server has a recovery key, must let the person type the code. A wrong code is found on the phone; only a right one activates the device, writes copy A, opens the vault and downloads the backup.

##### What was done

* **API:** `RecoveryKeyApi.activate(proof)` over `POST /backup/recovery-key/activate`. The errors become `RecoveryError` kinds `mismatch`, `tooMany`, `removed` and `noKey`.
* **`restoreWithCode.ts`:** parse the code, read the recovery key, derive RK, open the DEK on the phone (wrong code ends here, the server hears nothing), send the device proof, then wipe the old file, write copy A, open the vault, set the restore mark, `restoreAll`, clear the mark. RK, proof and DEK bytes are zeroed at the end.
* **Restore mark:** vault migration 2 creates `vault_state`; `restoreState.ts` sets, reads and clears `restore_pending`. After any open with the backup on, the lock controller resumes a restore that was cut short, in the background.
* **Lock controller:** new state `restoring`, new `restoreError` on `pendingDevice`, and `restoreWithCode(typed)`. Wired in `defaultLock.ts` with `restoreAll` from `defaultBackup.ts`.
* **UI:** `RestoreScreen.tsx` replaces the static pending text. Title, text, the "Usar mi código de recuperación" option, the code field, "Restaurar", the error texts and the "Cerrar sesión" link. Two comment slots mark where the next branch adds "Aprobar desde mi otro teléfono" and "No tengo ninguno de los dos".

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (126 suites, 1604 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Tests cover every outcome with fakes, including "a pending phone never reads records or opens the vault without the right code".

##### Decisions and problems

* **Partial result:** if the download fails after the vault opened (offline), `restore` answers `partial`, not a failure. The controller unlocks, the mark stays and the next open retries.
* **`restoring` keeps the screen:** the restore screen stays mounted in `pendingDevice` and `restoring`, so the typed code survives an error. The form is replaced by the spinner text.
* **Failed write or open after activation:** the vault is closed and copy A removed, so the phone is a pending-like phone again. Activating twice answers 204, so a retry works.
* **Wipe before write:** a left-over vault file cannot be read with the new key, so `vault.wipe()` runs after the activation and before copy A is written.
* **Field size:** one field of 26 characters in IBM Plex Mono at 15 px, not the six boxes of OnbCorreo.html at 22 px, so it fits the width.
* **Only a phone can check:** the real Argon2id time on a low-end device, the keyboard behaviour of the field, and the restore of a real backup.

##### Next step

Approval from the other phone and "No tengo ninguno de los dos" (SCRUM-63).

#### 2026-10-30 · "No tengo ninguno de los dos": start from zero

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/restore-without-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the last way out of the restore screen (HU-045): a person with neither the old phone nor the recovery code can start an empty vault after proving it is them with the email code password reset. The server decides (`POST /backup/reset`); the phone explains, calls it, and sets itself up as a first phone.

##### What was done

* **`adapters/api/backupResetApi.ts`:** `reset()` posts with `X-Device-Id` and no body. It maps 204 to ok, 403 `reset_not_allowed` to `notAllowed`, 403 `device_not_active` to `notActive`, a lost connection to `offline`. Contract tests cover each.
* **`application/resetBackup.ts`:** turns the API errors into `ok`, `notAllowed`, `notActive`, `offline` or `failed`.
* **`lockController.ts`:** `resetBackup()` calls it from a pending device and, on `ok`, runs `unlock()`. The phone is now active and the recovery key is gone, so `setUpFirstPhone` makes a new DEK and shows a new recovery code. A controller test checks the whole path.
* **UI:** the option "No tengo ninguno de los dos" opens `ResetExplanation` with the three paragraphs, "Empezar de cero" and "Volver". `notAllowed` shows the message and swaps the primary button for "Restablecer contraseña", which signs out through identity.
* **Tests:** the screen in light and dark, the flow with the controller, the API and the use case.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. This branch is stacked on `feature/restore-with-approval`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **"Empezar de cero" calls the server first**, because the person may have just done the email reset. Only `notAllowed` sends them to the password reset.
* **No direct reset-credentials entry in identity.** `changePassword` needs a session. So "Restablecer contraseña" calls `signOut()`; the person lands on Inicio and Keycloak's login page, which has "¿Olvidaste tu contraseña?". After the reset and a new sign in the phone returns to this screen, and "Empezar de cero" succeeds.
* **The option is shown to pending and active phones**, since both can be stuck without a key.
* **Only a phone can check:** the real Keycloak round trip, the 10 minute window, and that the new session counts as started after the reset.

#### 2026-11-03 · Send backup audit events the allow-list accepts and test them with the real validator

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-72. Branch: fix/backup-audit-meta-keys. PR: #244. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel asked for a check of what was left of M1 and M2, and for a plan to finish M2: "can you run a check in sonnet to see whats left of the M1 and M2 ... and if so then do a plan for it so it can be completed". The plan found one blocker, SCRUM-72. After Daniel said "continue", this branch carries the M2 half of the fix. It is stacked on `fix/audit-allow-backup-events` (M13).

##### What was done

* `services/api/internal/backup/recovery/service.go`: `recovery_key.set` no longer sends `kdf`.
* `services/api/internal/backup/records/service.go`: `backup.uploaded` sends `record_count` instead of `count`.
* `services/api/internal/backup/approvals/service.go`: approve and reject send `approver_device_id` instead of `approvedBy` and `rejectedBy`. Both still send `method=device_approval`.
* `services/api/internal/backup/reset/`: the local `AuditBackupReset` constant is removed, and the reset uses `audit.BackupReset` from the audit port.
* Tests:
  * The five fake recorders now call `audit.ValidateEvent` and return its error. Those are in the approvals, records, recovery and reset service tests, plus `recAudit` in `postgres/recovery_http_integration_test.go`, which every Postgres integration test uses. `ValidateEvent` is the same check the real audit store runs.
  * The unit tests now use UUID device ids, because the validator rejects ids like `"d1"`.
  * The assertions check the exact meta:
    * `recovery_key.set` has no meta;
    * `record_count` is `"2"`;
    * `approver_device_id` is the approver's id;
    * the reset type is `audit.BackupReset`.

##### Result

* Tests:
  * With only the fakes changed, 4 tests fail in approvals (`approvedBy` and `rejectedBy`), 5 in records (`count`) and 1 in recovery (`kdf`). This was checked again by stashing the service changes.
  * With the fix, `go test ./internal/backup/... ./internal/audit/...` passes, and `go build ./...` and `go vet`, with and without `-tags integration`, are clean.
* The Postgres integration tests were compiled but not run, because Docker was not running locally. CI runs them with `make test-integration`.
* Pull request: the top of the SCRUM-72 stack, above the M13 allow-list PR.
* Not checked yet: the flows on the local stack and on a second phone. That waits for both PRs to merge.

##### Decisions and problems

* The keys were renamed to ones the allow-list already accepts, so the M13 change stays small.
* `kdf` was dropped instead of being added to the list, because ADR 0002 fixes the Argon2id parameters, so the value never changes.
* The old fakes also hid a second problem: the test device ids were not UUIDs. Production ids come from Postgres and are UUIDs, so only the tests changed.
* Added after the PR opened: the first push failed CI's golangci-lint.
  * The fakes imported `internal/audit`, and depguard allows another module only through `internal/<module>/ports`, even in tests.
  * #243 moved the rules to `audit/ports.ValidateEvent`, and the fakes now call it through the `audit` port alias the tests already import.
  * golangci-lint v2.15.0 now passes on `./...`.
  * Linting with `--build-tags integration` still reports old issues in the Postgres integration tests, which import `internal/identity` to mount that module. CI does not lint with that tag, and those issues were already on develop.
* An audit failure still leaves the action stored, so the key is saved but the client gets a 500 (the "Efecto secundario" in SCRUM-72). With valid events this no longer happens in normal use, and it is out of scope here.

##### Next step

When both PRs are merged:

1. Run `make dev` and save the recovery code. Check that it answers 200 and that a `recovery_key.set` row lands in `audit.events`.
2. On a second phone, restore with the code, restore by approval, and reset.
3. Tick the Gate B row, then move SCRUM-62, SCRUM-63 and SCRUM-72 to "Finalizado".

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-96. Branch: feature/offer-biometrics-after-unlock. PR: #348. Status: partial.

##### What was done

* `lockController.ts`: a new `biometricOffer` state, entered only after a password unlock on the lock screen, and `finishBiometricOffer()`. The vault is already open behind it and it locks after 5 minutes away like the open app.
* `DekStore` port and `adapters/keychain/dekStore.ts`: a yes/no mark under `co.fynk.app.vault.biometricOffer`. `removeAll` removes it.
* `ui/BiometricOfferScreen.tsx`, new, with "Activar" and "Ahora no". `VaultFlow.tsx` shows it. Copy in `strings.es-CO.ts`.

* Tests: 2,779 pass in the whole mobile suite, `tsc` clean. ESLint and Prettier clean on the changed files.

* The mark is saved before the question shows, so a failure can never repeat it. If it cannot be saved, the person goes straight in.

#### 2026-11-09 · Tie the vault key to the account that created it

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-96. Branch: fix/vault-owner-binding. PR: #345. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel reported that creating an account and entering the Mailpit code sent them back to the first screen with an error: "no pudimos verificar tu contraseña, intenta de nuevo". Daniel asked why, and chose "Erase local, start fresh" for when a different account signs in on the same phone.

##### What was done

* `DekStore` port and `adapters/keychain/dekStore.ts`: `readOwner` and `writeOwner` under a new Keychain service, `co.fynk.app.vault.owner`. `removeAll` removes it too.
* `lockController.ts`: an owner check before any existing key is used, in `run()`, `unlockWithPassword()` and `unlockWithBiometrics()`. Another account's key is erased with `vault.wipe()` and the first phone path runs. The tag is written at setup and at every move to `unlocked`.
* `adapters/defaultLock.ts`: wires `userId` and `resetBackupState`.
* `CLAUDE.md`, "Where data lives": a row for the vault owner.
* Tests in `lockController.test.ts` (`vault owner`) and `dekStore.test.ts` (`owner tag`).

##### Result

* Tests: 906 pass in `src/modules/vault` and `src/modules/identity`, `tsc` clean. ESLint and Prettier clean on the changed files.
* On the Android emulator, with the whole stack and two test accounts: the second account got its own recovery code and an empty vault, with nothing from the first. The first account, back on the phone, got "Restaurar en un teléfono nuevo" and its recovery code worked, so its cloud backup was not overwritten.
* Pull request: #345.

##### Decisions and problems

* Rejecting another account on the lock screen is correct. Without that, anyone holding the phone could open the vault with their own login. The bugs were the wrong message and no way out (next pull request).
* The bug found on the way: the key was not tied to any account, so a second account signing in got the first account's vault.
* A phone with no tag is adopted by the current account, because phones set up before this fix have none.
* A session that ended during an unlock has no `userId`. The first version read that as "another account" and could erase the vault. It now fails closed with a keychain error and no wipe.
* A lock that arrived during the tag write left the app marked as unlocked over a closed vault. Fixed with a generation check and a test.

##### Next step

Sofia reviews the `CLAUDE.md` row, which is an M0 file.

#### 2026-10-29 · Onboarding step 1: Nombre

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-69. Branch: feature/onboarding-name-step. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the other onboarding logs of 29 Oct. The plan Sofia approved ends with branch 2, "steps 1 and 2", which became just Nombre once the email step was dropped (ADR 0007). For the name field Sofia said to keep the design's single field "but take the second word separated by whitespace as the 'Apellido'". It was built as: the first word is the first name and everything after it is the last name, so "Laura Gómez Ruiz" keeps both last names.

##### What was done

* **`domain/name.ts`:**
  * `splitFullName`, `joinName` and `greetingName`.
  * `nameProblem`, a copy of the API's `validName` rule:
    * 1 to 50 letters per part, of any script, counted in code points;
    * marks, spaces, apostrophes, hyphens and periods allowed;
    * starts with a letter and ends with a letter or a mark.
* **`adapters/api/profileNameApi.ts`:** reads the name with `GET /me` and saves it with `PATCH /me`, through the shared client and the identity contract. Nothing is imported from M1's internals, and the name travels only in the body.
* **`ui/NameStepScreen.tsx`:** content from `OnbNombre.html`.
  * The 88 px avatar with the 5 px ring in the theme's soft blue, which fixes the dark export bug.
  * "Hola, **Laura**!" from the first word, the field with the user icon and "Tu nombre", and the hint.
  * The field starts with the saved name, without ever replacing what the person typed.
  * "Continuar" stays disabled until the name is valid. It saves first and then opens Cuentas.
  * `invalid_profile` and network errors keep the person on the step with a message.
* **`StepLayout`:** a step can replace "Continuar" with its own save (`onContinue`, `busy`).
* **`index.ts`:** the flow uses the real Nombre step. Cuentas, Metas and Gastos are still placeholders.
* **`e2e/onboarding-complete.yaml`:** Maestro flow from Aviso to the Dashboard.
  * It skips Nombre, because the dev-token build has no API session.
  * It walks the placeholder steps, kills and reopens the app on step 3, then finishes.
  * It checks that the setup does not come back on the next launch.
* **Tests:**
  * `domain/name.test.ts` (24).
  * `adapters/api/profileNameApi.test.ts` (5): the body of `PATCH /me`, no query string, `invalid_profile`.
  * `ui/NameStepScreen.test.tsx` (10): disabled until valid, the greeting, the split saved before moving on, a refused name, network and API errors, the prefill, typed text not overwritten, and skipping.

##### Result

* Tests: the whole app passes, 81 suites and 1101 tests (118 snapshots). ESLint, `tsc` and Prettier are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet. Stacked on `feature/onboarding-intro-motion`.
* Maestro was not run: it is not installed on this computer and no emulator was available.
* The screens were not checked against the PNGs on a device from this session.

##### Decisions and problems

* **"J. Gómez" is refused.** The API rule says a part must end with a letter, so a first name like "J." fails. The phone follows the API exactly so it never sends a name the API refuses. If the team wants initials, the API rule changes first (M1).
* **The validation messages are not in the design:** new copy, needs design review.
* **`Avatar` size 88 draws 32 px initials** (shared map), while the design says 30. Left as the shared component decides.

#### 2026-10-29 · Keep the onboarding progress in the vault and resume it

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-69. Branch: feature/onboarding-progress. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia gave Claude Code the M3 prompt ("You are building module M3 Guided onboarding of Fynk. This module owns the wizard and its navigation only."). Before any code, Claude Code checked `develop` and asked three questions. Sofia chose:

* **4 steps, no Correo.** ADR 0007 verifies the email with a code at sign up, so the email step and `EmailVerificationStep` no longer exist.
* **The header "Omitir" skips only that step.** The warning appears from "Omitir por ahora" and after skipping every step.
* **One name field, like the design.** "take the second word separated by whitespace as the Apellido".

Scope of the session: the wizard shell and the Nombre step. The shell was split into this branch (rules and storage) and `feature/onboarding-wizard-shell` (screens) to keep pull requests small.

##### What was done

* **`domain/steps.ts`:** the four steps `name`, `accounts`, `goals` and `recurring`, with "Paso N de 4", next and previous.
* **`domain/progress.ts`:** pure rules of the setup:
  * `start`, `completeStep` and `skipStep`. `skipStep` reports `showWarning` when the last step is skipped after skipping all the others.
  * `visit` saves the screen on top, after the back button or the back gesture.
  * `skipAll` and `finish`, and `screensUpTo` for the resume stack.
  * A finished setup never moves again.
* **`adapters/vault/migrations.ts`:** table `onboarding_progress` (one row: step, completed and skipped steps as JSON, `finished_at`, `updated_at`).
* **`adapters/vault/vaultProgressStore.ts`:** load and upsert. Step ids this build does not know are dropped, so an old saved setup never breaks the app.
* **`application/onboardingService.ts`:** an external store, ready for React's `useSyncExternalStore`.
  * It reads the progress on `vault.unlocked` and forgets it on `vault.locked`.
  * It saves every move before showing it, and runs one move at a time.
  * It reports `failed` when the vault cannot be read for another reason.
* **Tests:**
  * 27 domain tests.
  * 4 vault contract tests on sql.js.
  * 11 service tests, including "reopens on step 4 after the app is killed on step 4" and "shows the warning before ending when every step was skipped".

##### Result

* Tests: 3 suites, 42 tests pass. ESLint and `tsc` are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet. Stacked on `refactor/share-screen-options`.
* Jira SCRUM-69: the criterion now reads "Pasos: nombre, cuentas y efectivo, metas, gastos recurrentes (el correo se verifica en el registro, ADR 0007)", with a comment explaining the change.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Steps are stored by id, not by number,** so a later change of order does not move a saved setup to the wrong screen.
* **The saved step follows the screen on top** (`visit`). The back gesture can pop a screen without our button, so a separate "back" move would miss it.
* **Finding: the progress is not part of the encrypted backup.** A person who restores on a new phone would see the setup again. The team should decide whether a restore marks it as finished.

##### Next step

The screens of the shell: Aviso, the step frame, Omitir and the gate (`feature/onboarding-wizard-shell`).

#### 2026-10-27 · Consent domain rules (M4 step 1, part 1 of 4)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-20, SCRUM-21. Branch: feature/consents-domain-rules. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

First M4 session. The developer asked to read the whole developer kit first and to treat it as the source of every criterion, decision and procedure. Then: "Let's start by the first branch in the work plan and then we'll work progressively towards completing the whole M4." The first branch is `feature/consents-backend` (schema, endpoints, real `ConsentChecker`, expiry job, audit). It came out to about 2,100 lines, so the developer chose to split it into 4 stacked branches. This is the first one.

##### What was done

* `internal/consents/domain/consent.go`: `Scope`, `Status`, `Text`, `Evidence`, `Consent` and `Request`.
  * `NewPending` checks that the consent is specific (known provider; a non-empty subset of its scopes with no repeats; the purpose and the 365-day term shown on Conectar banco; the text version and the hash of the text the app showed; device id; app version; locale `es-CO`) and stores the acceptance evidence.
  * `Grant` turns a pending consent active only with complete evidence, the same text version and hash, and the bank's consent id. `expires_at = granted_at + term_days`.
  * `StatusAt` and `ActiveAt` report an active consent past its term as expired, even before the daily job writes it.
* `domain/errors.go`: one stable problem code per broken rule (`invalid_scopes`, `purpose_mismatch`, `term_mismatch`, `unknown_text_version`, `text_mismatch`, `missing_evidence` and others).
* Tests from the criteria: what was shown is what is recorded (HU-006), no open or general consents, no activation without evidence and a known text version (module test), and expiry after `term_days` with a fixed time (HU-007 and module test).

##### Result

* Tests: 7 tests (13 rejection cases) pass; `go vet` and golangci-lint are clean.
* Pull request: opened right after this log, based on `chore/add-oapi-codegen-runtime`.

##### Decisions and problems

* The purpose, the 12-month term and locale `es-CO` are constants taken from the M4 prompt. Changing them needs the team (prompt, "Ask before").
* The scopes each provider supports are listed in the domain until M5 exposes its provider list through `bank/ports`. All three providers support the three scopes.
* Go, pnpm (corepack) and the JS dependencies were not installed on this Mac. With the developer's approval they were installed as `docs/local-dev.md` says.

##### Next step

`feature/consents-schema-and-store`: Postgres schema, seed of text `2026-10-v1` and the store.

#### 2026-10-27 · Consent text and consent endpoints (M4 step 1, part 4 of 4)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-20, SCRUM-21. Branch: feature/consents-endpoints. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as `feature/consents-domain-rules`. Last of the 4 stacked branches of `feature/consents-backend`. With it, step 1 of the M4 work plan is complete.

##### What was done

* `contracts/modules/consents.yaml` (contract first, then `make generate`), each with an example for Prism:
  * `GET /consent-texts/current`: text, its SHA-256, purpose and 365-day term, so Conectar banco shows the scope and the term (HU-006).
  * `POST /consents`: needs `X-Device-Id`, accepts `Idempotency-Key`, `201` pending, `422` with the rule's code.
  * `GET /consents` and `GET /consents/{id}`: status, `grantedAt`, `expiresAt` as of now (HU-007).
* `internal/consents/server.go`: implements the generated strict server. Rule errors become problem JSON with their own code (422, 404 `consent_not_found`, 409 `consent_not_pending`); anything else is a 500 without details.
* `internal/consents/module.go`: every route goes through `auth.Require` before the generated server parses the request, except the public example health path. With a database, `New` starts the daily expiry job; `cmd/migrate` builds the modules without one, so it never starts it.
* `internal/app/app_test.go` (M0's file, approved by the developer, Sofia asked to review): removed the two consents paths from the list of routes that must answer 501.
* HTTP tests: token required before parsing, text with purpose and term, create then get and list, other users hidden, 422 with its code, idempotency.

##### Result

* Tests: 6 HTTP tests pass; `make lint` and `make test` pass (313 mobile tests plus every Go and script test).
* By hand against the real API and Postgres 16 in Docker: the text hash matches its body, create gives `pending`, get and list return it, a wrong term gives `422 term_mismatch`, no token gives `401`, the evidence lands in the row.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.

##### Decisions and problems

* The example health path stays, still answering `stub`, because M0's `TestGeneratedHealthPathsArePublicAndAnswerFromTheContract` expects it for every module. Removing it is left to M0.
* Revocation (`POST /consents/{id}/revoke`) is not in this step. It is step 2, `feature/consent-revocation-with-provider`.

##### What was done

* `internal/consents/migrations/1_create_consents`: tables `consent_texts` and `consents` as written in the M4 prompt, plus:
  * an `idempotency_key` column, unique per user, so `POST /consents` honours `Idempotency-Key` (shared contracts, section 1);
  * checks: `status` in the five values, at least one scope, the five evidence keys present, and an `active` row always with `granted_at`, `expires_at` and `provider_consent_id`;
  * indexes for the user's list and for the expiry job.
* `2_seed_consent_text`: text `2026-10-v1`, written only from the Conectar banco copy in the M4 prompt (receiver, data, purpose, term, what Fynk can never do, revocation, checkbox). Postgres computes the SHA-256.
* `store/postgres.go`: texts, insert with idempotency, get, list newest first, activate only while pending, and expire due consents with one `UPDATE ... RETURNING`, so each consent is reported once even with several API instances.
* `consents/module.go`: the module returns its migrations (routes are still the stub).
* `cmd/migrate/main_test.go` (M0's file, approved by the developer, Sofia asked to review): the test assumed no module has SQL. It now checks that exactly the schemas with SQL try to connect to the unreachable database.
* Integration tests (`-tags integration`, real Postgres 16): seeded text and hash, round trip with grant and expiry once, idempotency per user, the database refuses an active consent without its grant and a consent without evidence, a non-UUID id is not found.

##### Result

* Tests: unit suite passes; the 5 store integration tests pass against `postgres:16.15-alpine` in Docker; `go run ./cmd/migrate up` applies version 2 and a second run changes nothing. golangci-lint is clean.

##### What was done

* `internal/consents/service/service.go`:
  * `Create`: records the explicit acceptance as a pending consent. The same `Idempotency-Key` from the same user returns the first consent; a different request with that key is `idempotency_conflict`.
  * `List` and `Get`: only the user's own consents (another user's is "not found"), newest first, with the status as of now (HU-007, "información actualizada").
  * `Active` and `MarkGranted`: the real `ports.ConsentChecker` (shared contracts, section 4.5). `Active` is false for anything not active, past its term or owned by another user.
  * `ExpireDue` and `RunExpiry`: the expiry job, run at once and then every interval until its context ends.
  * Audit: one event per state change (`consent.created`, `consent.granted`, `consent.expired`) with `provider` and `consent_id` only, plus the user and device ids.
  * IDs are UUID v7 (shared contracts, section 1).
* `ports/ports.go`: `New(deps)` returns the real checker, so M5 builds it from the `Deps` it already receives, without importing M4 internals. M0's `LocalChecker` stays.
* `store/memory.go`: in-memory store with the same rules, for tests.
* Tests from the criteria: pending with evidence (HU-006), list and status as of now (HU-007), no activation without evidence or a known text (module test), expiry after `term_days` with a fixed clock, audited once (module test), one audit row per change with no amounts, emails or tokens (module test), idempotency, other users hidden.

##### Result

* Tests: 14 service tests pass with `-race`; the whole Go suite and golangci-lint pass.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.
* The branch is about 640 lines, 300 of them tests. It is the smallest split that keeps the use cases and their tests together.

##### Decisions and problems

* A failing audit store is logged and does not undo the change. The change is already committed and the consent row keeps its own dates (`created_at`, `granted_at`, `expires_at`), so the evidence is not lost, and M5 never sees a failure after the bank already gave tokens. M13 is asked to confirm this in review.
* `Active` already treats an ended term as inactive, so the daily job only keeps the stored status and the `consent.expired` audit current.

`feature/consents-endpoints`: the contract and the HTTP endpoints.

#### 2026-10-28 · Revoke a consent and notify the entity (M4 step 2)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-22, SCRUM-20. Branch: feature/consent-revocation-with-provider. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After M5 step 1, the developer asked to continue with M4 step 2 and whether M4 would be complete after it. Key sentence: "Continue with M4 Step 2. Before doing so, after that is done, is M4 complete or is there anything missing from it?" The answer, given before starting: the backend is complete except for the bank wiring, which comes with M5 step 2's signer. Steps 3 and 4 (mobile) and the Gate C check on staging remain.

##### What was done

* Branch stacked on #46 (M4's endpoints) and #52 (the bank port). Their edits to M0's `app_test.go` conflicted as expected; the merge removes both modules' paths.
* Contract: `POST /consents/{id}/revoke` (`200` revoked, `404`, `409 consent_not_revocable`) and `providerNotifiedAt` on every consent.
* Domain:
  * `Revoke` ends an active or pending consent at once; revoking twice returns it unchanged;
  * an expired or failed consent cannot be revoked;
  * `NoticeFailed` schedules the next try in 10 minutes, and none after 24 hours;
  * `NewPending` now receives the provider's scopes, which the service reads from `bank/ports`.
* Service:
  * `Revoke` stores the revocation, audits `consent.revoked`, then tells the entity right away (5 s limit);
  * `NotifyDue` and `RunNotify` are the retry job, checking every minute;
  * `BankRevoker` reaches M5's installed revoker at call time.
* Store: migration 3 adds `provider_notified_at`, `next_notify_at` and `notify_attempts` to the consent row, plus a check that a revoked consent has its date. `ClaimNotices` uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`.
* `bank/ports` (M5, same developer): `InstallRevoker` and `InstalledRevoker`, the same pattern as `auth.Install`. Until M5 step 2 installs the real revoker, every notice fails with `provider_unavailable` and is retried.
* Tests:
  * the HU-008 criteria;
  * the module test: after revoke, `Active` is false at once and a stubbed M5 query answers `409 consent_revoked`;
  * retries every 10 minutes and stopping after 24 hours;
  * revoking twice notifies once, and a pending consent is revoked without calling the entity;
  * only the owner can revoke;
  * scopes come from `bank/ports`;
  * Postgres tests for revoke, claim and confirm.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass. The 8 store integration tests pass on Postgres 16 in Docker.
* By hand against the real API and Postgres: `cmd/migrate` reaches version 3. Revoking gives `revoked` at once; revoking again gives the same consent; the notice fails (no revoker installed yet) and is scheduled to retry.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.
* Size: about 880 lines, about 430 of them tests.

##### Decisions and problems

* The notice state lives on the consent row, not in a new table. It is consent metadata, which "Where data lives" already keeps in the cloud.
* The first manual run showed the error code as `[REDACTED]`, because M0's logger masks any key named `code` (it treats it as an authorization code). The key is now `error_code`.
* The real notice reaches Banco Demo only after M5 step 2 installs the revoker with the `private_key_jwt` signer.
* Bancolombia consents are refused now, because it is not in M5's provider list until its spike.

##### Next step

M5 step 2: `feature/bank-link-with-par-and-pkce`, which installs the real revoker.
