#### 2026-10-29 · Let Jest exit after M4's tests

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-21. Branch: fix/consents-jest-open-handle. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Part of step A that the developer approved ("let's start with A which is quick"). The handoff said Jest prints "a worker process has failed to exit gracefully" on M4 and M5's suites, and asked to "check whether one of our suites leaks a timer".

##### What was done

* Ran each of the 13 M4 and M5 suites alone with `--detectOpenHandles` and a 90 second limit. Twelve exit in 0 to 2 seconds. `consents/application/useConsents.test.tsx` passes but never exits.
* Cause: its test `QueryClient` sets `gcTime: Infinity` only for mutations. After the hooks unmount, each query starts React Query's default 5 minute cleanup timer, which keeps the process alive.
* Fix: `gcTime: Infinity` for queries too, as the other M4 and M5 suites already do. Test code only.

##### Result

* Tests: the suite exits in about 1 second. M4 and M5 together: 13 suites, 130 tests pass, and the worker warning is gone.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

#### 2026-11-05 · Fail consents still pending after 24 hours

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-20. Branch: feature/consents-fail-stale-pending. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Juan Carlos decided what happens to consents nobody finished: "If a consent is still pendant after 24 hours, that should be marked as failed, for sure or even less, beacuse in anyway, the own person can ask for another consent".

##### What was done

* `consents/domain`: `PendingFor = 24 * time.Hour`. `StatusAt` reports a pending consent as `failed` once it passes, so `Get`, `List`, `Active`, `Grant` and `Revoke` treat it as ended even before the job runs.
* `consents/store`: `FailPending(createdBy)` in Postgres and in memory, which marks those rows `failed` and returns each one once.
* `consents/service`: `FailStalePending`, run by the existing daily job (`RunExpiry`) after `ExpireDue`.
* Tests: a domain test (pending until the last second, then failed; it can no longer be granted or revoked), 2 service tests (only the old pending one fails, once; reads show it failed before the job; the job runs it), and a Postgres test of `FailPending`.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./internal/consents/... ./internal/bank/... ./internal/privacy/...` passes; the consents integration tests pass against Postgres 16; golangci-lint: 0 issues.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* 24 hours, not less. Conectar banco keeps one idempotency key per entity while the screen is open, so "Intentar de nuevo" reuses the same pending consent. With a short limit, a person who left the screen open and retried later would get a consent that had already failed, and the retry would keep failing until they left the screen. The bank authorization itself lasts only 10 minutes, so 24 hours is never too short.
* M5's `POST /bank/authorizations` already refuses a consent that is not pending, so a failed one can't start an authorization.
* No audit event for this change yet: M13's audit list has no `consent.failed` type, and adding it means changing `internal/audit` (Juan Pablo's module). Asked Juan Carlos whether to propose it.
* The phone already shows `failed` as "Fallido".

##### Next step

Decide with Juan Carlos whether to propose `consent.failed` to Juan Pablo.

#### 2026-11-05 · Test RevokeAll and record the account_deleted reason

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/consents-revoke-all-reason. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Juan Carlos approved the recommendation to test the `ConsentsRevoker.RevokeAll` that Juan Pablo added in #223 for account deletion, and to record a reason: "Do the revoke all".

##### What was done

* `consents/domain`: new reason `ReasonAccountDeleted = "account_deleted"`.
* `consents/service`: `RevokeAll(ctx, userID, reason)` revokes each active and pending consent through the same path as `Revoke` (audit `consent.revoked`, entity notice, 24 hour retries). `Revoke` now calls the same private `revoke` with `user_request`.
* `consents/ports`: `consentsRevoker.RevokeAll` calls the service with `account_deleted`. The `ConsentsRevoker` interface and `NewConsentsRevoker` stay the same, so M13's code does not change.
* Tests: 3 service tests (active and pending revoked with the reason; already revoked, expired and other users' consents untouched; only the active one notifies the entity; an unreachable entity still revokes and schedules the retry; a user without consents), 1 port test (the reason and errors pass through), and 1 integration test of `NewConsentsRevoker` against Postgres.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./internal/consents/... ./internal/privacy/...` passes. The integration tests of `consents/ports` and `consents/store` pass against Postgres 16. golangci-lint: 0 issues, with and without the `integration` tag.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* The reason is stored in the consent's `revoked_reason` column, not in the audit event: the M4 prompt says consent audit events carry the provider and the consent id only. The column is in clear and auditable.
* The old loop treated `ErrAlreadyRevoked` as an error to skip, but `Revoke` never returns it. The new loop skips `ErrNotRevocable` instead, for a consent whose term ended between the list and the revoke.
* `make test-integration` still does not run the M4 and M5 store tests (asked of Sofia before), so CI does not run the new integration test either.

##### Next step

Tell Juan Pablo (`@juancontrerasp`) in the pull request that account deletion now records `account_deleted`.

#### 2026-10-28 · Banco Demo adapter and egress client (M5 step 1, part 2 of 3)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44, SCRUM-45. Branch: feature/bank-demo-adapter. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Continue M5 step 1 after the egress settings PR. Key sentence: "proceed so that we can finish step 1 of the order you propose."

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/egress`: the HTTP client every bank adapter uses. It trusts only `EGRESS_CA_FILE`, needs TLS 1.2 or newer, never follows a redirect (a token can never be sent elsewhere), and times out after 15 s. `BaseURL` builds `<EGRESS_URL>/<route>` and fails when the egress is not configured.
* `internal/bank/adapters/bankdemo`: `ports.Provider` against `contracts/bank-simulator.yaml`, for two providers on one simulator:
  * Banco Demo keeps savings, credit card and CDT; Billetera Demo keeps the wallet. This matches the onboarding design (Banco Demo 3.200.000, Billetera Demo 4.600.000).
  * Accounts map to the shared shape:
    * centavos, with decimal strings converted exactly, never with floats;
    * only the last 4 digits;
    * a card's debt as a positive amount owed (the simulator reports it as negative);
    * the card's next payment date computed in Bogotá time;
    * CDT rate and dates;
    * unknown product types are skipped.
  * Movements map with booked or pending status, merchant and bank category. Pages follow the cursor, 200 a page.
  * OAuth:
    * PAR with PKCE S256, `state`, `nonce`, Fynk's consent id and a `private_key_jwt` assertion; only `client_id` and `request_uri` travel in the authorize URL;
    * code exchange and refresh return the bank's `consent_id`;
    * revocation uses a client credentials token, and a consent the bank no longer knows counts as done.
  * Bank answers become stable errors (`consent_revoked`, `invalid_grant`, `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`, `provider_unavailable`) and never keep the bank's text.
* Tests against a fake server that follows the simulator's contract, plus TLS tests with a second CA.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass.
* Pull request: opened right after this log, stacked on #52 and #53.
* Size: about 1,120 lines, about 490 of them tests.

##### Decisions and problems

* The adapter is not wired into the endpoints yet: they answer `501` until M5 step 2 adds authorizations and links.
* Reading the simulator found more points for M16 besides the three in #52:
  * there is no browser authorization page yet, so the full link flow cannot run end to end;
  * consent ids contain `-`, so the refresh grant reads the wrong consent;
  * the public listener (8443, published as `localhost:8082`) serves the same API and OAuth routes without mTLS;
  * the card debt is negative in `fixtures/demo-user.json` while the shared contract says it is positive.

  They go to the M16 owner in part 3, where the tests against the simulator show them.
* A test first trusted a second server by mistake, because every `httptest` TLS server shares one certificate. The test now gives that server its own certificate.

##### Next step

Part 3: contract tests against the running simulator through Caddy, and the mTLS test with and without the client certificate.

The phone has to open that exact URL. `react-native-app-auth` builds its own authorization URL, with its own `state` and PKCE. `openAuthSessionAsync` opens our URL as is, in the same system browser session (ASWebAuthenticationSession on iOS, Custom Tabs on Android), so it's still never a webview.
* **No `app.json` change:** the package's config plugin only does something with the optional `experimentalLauncherActivity` setting, which we don't use.
* **A new dev build is needed** once code uses the native module. Sofia's call when to rebuild.

  * `base64Url`;
  * `challengeOf` (SHA-256 from `@noble/hashes`, as M2's domain already uses);
  * `createPkce(random)`: a 43 character verifier from 32 random bytes, and a state from 16;
  * `newNonce(random)`: 32 characters.
  * The random bytes are injected, so the domain stays pure.
* `domain/callback.ts`: `BANK_CALLBACK_URL` (`fynk://bank/callback`) and `readCallback(url, expectedState)`. It returns:
  * `code`;
  * `denied` (`access_denied`);
  * `failed` (other bank errors);
  * `state_mismatch`;
  * `invalid` (another URL, or no code or error).
  * The state is checked before the code or the error is trusted. It parses the query by hand, because React Native's `URL` has no `searchParams`.
* `adapters/bankApi.ts` with the `BankApiPort` port: `authorize`, `exchange`, `accounts`, `transactions` (cursor) and `refresh`.
* Tests:
  * PKCE against the RFC 7636 Appendix B vector, plus unique values and lengths;
  * 6 callback cases;
  * the adapter on a fake fetch: request bodies, no token in any URL, a new nonce per call, and `consent_revoked` coming through as `ApiError.code`.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 10 suites, 52 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 475 lines, more than half tests.
* Nothing calls the adapter yet. Step B4 wires it to Conectar banco.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Contract mismatch in our own module:** `contracts/modules/bank.yaml` says the nonce is 16 to 64 characters, and the backend (`service/queries.go`) accepts 16 to 128. The phone sends 32, which fits both. To fix later in a contract PR of ours: change `maxLength` to 128, or the backend to 64.
* **A mistake in the first test,** caught by the test itself: the RFC verifier string was typed wrong. Node's `crypto` confirmed that the code and the RFC's octets and challenge agree, and the test was fixed.
* **The verifier and state stay in memory** during the browser handoff (B4), never in the vault or a log.

`feature/mobile-connect-bank-flow`.

#### 2026-10-29 · Consent-checked bank queries, refresh, nonces and rate limit (M5 step 2, part 8)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44, SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/bank-link-queries. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as `2026-10-29-juanleal05-m5-bank-link-settings.md`. Key sentence: "Then let's continue with M5 step 2 as better as possible." The developer also asked to look at the CI error of #131.

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/service/queries.go`: before every bank call, a trusted device, 30 queries a minute per user, the link of that user and device, and the consent still active. When the consent ended (in Fynk or at the bank) the link ends and the answer is 409 `consent_revoked`.
  * `Accounts` audits `bank.sync` with the accounts count; failures audit `bank.sync_failed` with the error code.
  * `Refresh` needs the link's current refresh token (its fingerprint), then rotates it; `bank.token_refreshed`.
  * The bank's own 429 is passed back with its `Retry-After`.
* `server.go` and `module.go`: the query and refresh endpoints, 429 with `Retry-After`, and a `X-Request-Nonce` check on every `POST /bank/*` (400 `nonce_required`, 409 `replayed_request`).
* Tests: a refresh token of another link is rejected, the old token stops working after a refresh, a revoked consent answers 409 and ends the link, another user or device gets `link_not_found`, the 31st query gets 429, a replayed nonce is rejected; and the same over HTTP.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass on the whole stack. The Postgres and Redis tests pass against Postgres 16 and Redis 7 in Docker.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The #131 CI "failure" was not a code problem.** `gh stack submit` starts two CI runs for the same commit when it opens a pull request. The workflow's concurrency rule cancels one, and its `CI passed` job fails on purpose for a cancelled run. The other run passed, and it is the one that counts. It can be avoided in M0's workflow (for example, skip `CI passed` when the run itself was cancelled). That is Sofia's call, so nothing was changed.
* **`link_id` is not in M13's allow-list for `bank.sync_failed`,** so that event carries only the provider, the error code and the status.
* **Banco Demo has no authorization page yet,** so the whole link cannot run end to end. Everything is tested against a fake provider and the stores against real Postgres and Redis.

Review and merge this stack bottom-up. Then M5 step 3 (the phone opens the portal and keeps the tokens), once M2's vault and M16's authorization page exist; and the Bancolombia spike.

#### 2026-10-29 · Keep bank links and tokens in the phone's vault

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/bank-vault-tables. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second part of step B, which the developer approved ("Yes, push and do B"): the `bank_links` and `bank_tokens` vault tables, a small store, and the subscription to M4's `onConsentRevoked` that deletes a revoked link's tokens.

##### What was done

* `domain/link.ts`: `NewBankLink`, `BankLink` (status, `lastSyncedAt`, `lastAttemptAt`, `revokedAt`) and `BankTokens`.
* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts`: version 1 with `bank_links` (one link per consent, `consent_id` unique) and `bank_tokens` (one row per link). Registered with `registerVaultMigrations('bank', …)`.
* `adapters/vault/vaultLinkStore.ts` with the `LinkStore` port:
  * `saveLink` writes the link and its tokens in one transaction;
  * `getLinks` never returns tokens;
  * `getTokens` returns only an active link's tokens;
  * `updateTokens` ignores a revoked link;
  * `revokeLink` and `revokeByConsent` keep the first revoke date and delete the tokens in the same transaction.
* `application/linkRevocation.ts`: on M4's event, revokes the consent's links, at the API's time or else the clock's.
* `index.ts`: registers the tables, subscribes to `onConsentRevoked`, and exports `getLinks()` and the `BankLink` type.
* Tests:
  * the store on a real SQLite vault (sql.js), 8 tests;
  * the handler, 3 tests;
  * `index.test.ts`: the wiring end to end on the in-memory vault, plus a source check that no M5 file registers a backup collection.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 7 suites, 34 tests. Full mobile suite 114 suites, 1,370 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 480 lines, about half of them tests.
* The phone side of HU-008's "Sin nuevas sincronizaciones" now works for links. Nothing creates a link yet (step B4 does).

##### Decisions and problems

* **`last_attempt_at` is in the table now,** so step 4's 60 second cooldown needs no second migration. It counts from the start of a sync, finished or not.
* **A locked vault makes the listener fail,** and M4 already ignores listener errors. The next sync gets `409 consent_revoked` from the backend and revokes the link then (step 4).
* **M6's `markLinkRevoked` is not called yet:** M6's stack (#164 → #167) isn't merged. Step 4 calls it when M6 lands.
* **Tokens are stored as vault text.** The vault is SQLCipher, so they're encrypted at rest. They're never logged and never backed up.

`feature/bank-link-api-and-pkce`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The link flow on the phone, through the system browser

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/mobile-connect-bank-flow. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Fourth part of step B ("Yes, push and do B"): after "Ir al portal", create the authorization, open the bank in the system browser, check `state`, exchange the code, save the tokens in the vault, then read the accounts, with clear errors for every case.

##### What was done

* `application/linkBank.ts`: `linkBank(deps, { provider, consentId })`:
  * creates PKCE and the state, then `POST /bank/authorizations`;
  * opens the `authorizeUrl` as is, and checks the return with `readCallback`;
  * exchanges the code with the verifier and saves the link and tokens in one vault transaction, with the expiry from `expiresIn` and the clock;
  * then `POST /bank/accounts/query`.
* Outcomes:
  * `linked` (with the accounts, an empty list, or `null` if only the accounts query failed);
  * `cancelled` (the browser was closed);
  * `denied`;
  * `failed` with a stable reason: `state_mismatch`, `bank_error`, `expired` (`authorization_not_found`), `already_linked`, `unavailable`, `offline`, `device_required` or `unknown`.
* `adapters/systemBrowser.ts`: `openAuthSessionAsync` with `preferEphemeralSession`, and `secureRandom` from `expo-crypto`.
* Tests:
  * 13 for the flow on a real SQLite vault: the verifier sent at the exchange matches the challenge sent first; a wrong state exchanges nothing; nothing is stored when any step fails; the link stays if only the accounts query fails; every error code maps to its reason;
  * 6 for the browser adapter.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 12 suites, 71 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 340 lines.
* Not yet on screen: the next layer wires it to Conectar banco and shows the result.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The screen comes in its own layer,** so this PR stays near 340 lines and the next one is only UI.
* **Ephemeral browser session:** the bank's cookies aren't kept after the link (iOS). The person logs in at the bank every time they link. That's the safer default for a bank login.
* **The accounts shown right after linking aren't stored.** M6's `upsertFromSync` isn't merged, and storing accounts is the sync's job (step 4, first sync of 12 months).
* **Open risk:** if the exchange works but the vault write fails (for example, the vault locked at that moment), the backend has an active link the phone has no token for. The person sees `unknown` and can revoke the consent and link again. A retry isn't possible: the code is single-use.
* **Only a phone can check:** that Android's Custom Tab return reaches `openAuthSessionAsync`, and that React Navigation ignores `fynk://bank/callback` (no screen has that path).
* **End to end against Banco Demo isn't possible yet:** the simulator has no authorization page (M16, Juan Pablo).

##### Next step

`feature/connect-bank-result-screen`: the screen states, `ConnectBankButton` and `BankLinkList`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The phone's sync engine

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-24, SCRUM-25, SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/mobile-sync-engine. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second layer of step C ("After that, start with C"): for each active link, refresh if needed, read the accounts and each account's movements by cursor, write them through M6 and M7 with no duplicates on the 3-day overlap, emit `sync.completed` or `sync.failed`, and end the link on `409 consent_revoked`.

##### What was done

* `application/syncEngine.ts`: `createSyncEngine(deps).syncAll()`. For each active link:
  * the 60 s cooldown, then `markAttempt`;
  * renew the access token if under 60 s are left (the new tokens are kept);
  * `POST /bank/accounts/query`, then each account's movements by cursor from `syncSince`;
  * accounts to M6 through `AccountsSink`, movements to M7 through `TransactionsSink`;
  * `markSynced`.
* Results per link: `synced`, `cooldown`, `revoked` or `failed`, with the API's code.
  * `consent_revoked` and `link_not_found` end the link on the phone: revoked, tokens deleted, M6's `markLinkRevoked`.
  * `rate_limited` stops asking for the remaining links.
  * Calls made during a run share it. A locked vault (`VaultLockedError`) does nothing.
* Events: `sync.failed` per failed or revoked link, and one `sync.completed` with the synced link ids, the new movement ids and the changed account ids.
* `application/ports.ts`: `AccountsSink` (M6's shape) and `TransactionsSink` (the writer M5 needs from M7). `LinkStore` gets `markAttempt` and `markSynced`.
* Tests: 12 for the engine on a real SQLite vault with fakes for the API, M6 and M7, plus 1 for the store marks. They cover:
  * 12 months first;
  * two pulls within 60 s make one network call;
  * a shared run;
  * the 3-day overlap gives no new ids;
  * cursor paging;
  * token renewal;
  * revocation (both codes);
  * a revoked link is never synced;
  * a failure keeps the last update date;
  * `rate_limited`;
  * a locked vault.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 16 suites, 113 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 500 lines, about 260 of them tests. Over the 400 guideline; the engine and its tests are kept together.
* Not wired yet: nothing calls the engine. The next layer adds the triggers.

##### Decisions and problems

* **M7 has no writer for synced movements.** `TransactionsSink` is the shape M5 needs: upsert by `(accountId, providerTxId)`, delete the pending movements since `since` that the bank no longer returns, and return the new ids. Sofia (M7) decides whether M7 offers this or something else.
* **`refresh_token_mismatch` is a failure, not a revocation:** the link stays, and the person sees the error. If it keeps happening, they revoke and link again.
* **The movement count isn't audited on the backend** (`bank.sync` has `accounts_count` only). The phone knows the count, but the backend only sees pages. Left as it is.
* **The movements of every account type are asked for, CDTs too.** If Banco Demo answers with an error for a CDT, that link fails; to watch once the simulator runs end to end.

##### Next step

`feature/sync-triggers-and-feedback`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The sync rules and the mapping to Account and Transaction

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-24, SCRUM-25. Branch: feature/sync-rules. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer asked to push step 3 and "After that, start with C" (M5 step 4, the sync engine). First layer: the pure rules, with an injected clock.

##### What was done

* `domain/syncRules.ts`:
  * `canStartSync`: 60 s per link, counted from the last start; a clock that went back never locks a link;
  * `shouldSyncOnForeground`: 15 minutes or more in the background;
  * `syncSince`: 12 calendar months back on the first sync (29 February goes to 28 February), else 3 days before the last sync;
  * `needsRefresh`: less than 60 s left on the access token.
* `domain/syncMapping.ts`:
  * `toAccount` and `toTransaction` map the bank's shapes to the shared `Account` and `Transaction` (contracts, section 2);
  * `accountIdOf` and `transactionIdOf` derive stable ids from the bank's own ids (SHA-256, length-prefixed parts);
  * movements get `merchantKey` with `normalizeMerchant`, and stay as `other` with `categorySource: 'rule'` for M7;
  * an unknown provider is refused.
* Tests: 10 for the rules, 7 for the mapping.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 15 suites, 100 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 330 lines.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Stable ids:** M6's `upsertFromSync` keeps the id of the first row it sees for each `(provider, providerAccountId)`. If M5 made a new id on each sync, movements would point to an account id M6 never stored. Deriving the id from the bank's ids avoids that, and gives M7 the same movement id on the 3-day overlap.
* **"After 15 minutes" means 15 minutes in the background.** The prompt says "AppState back to active after 15 minutes", which could also mean since the last sync. The 60 s cooldown still applies on top. To confirm with the team if they meant otherwise.
* **12 months are calendar months,** not 365 days.

##### Next step

`feature/mobile-sync-engine`.

  * `syncNow()` (calls made during a run share it);

* `application/useSync.ts`: `useSync()` over the controller (`useSyncExternalStore`).
* `domain/syncRules.ts`: `bogotaDay`.
* `strings.es-CO.ts`: "<Entidad> sincronizada", "Tus movimientos están al día." (M5 prompt).
* `index.ts`: exports `syncNow`, `useSync` and the `SyncReport` types; starts the triggers on `appEvents` and `AppState`; the entity names come from `GET /providers`, kept in memory.
* Tests: 9 for the controller, plus 1 in `index.test.ts`. They cover:
  * the unlock;
  * 15 minutes in the background, not before;
  * stopping;
  * the syncing and idle states with the last date;
  * one notice per day (Bogotá day at 23:30);
  * no notice on failure or cooldown;
  * a notice failure;
  * no engine, so no bank calls.

##### Result

* Tests: full mobile suite 124 suites, 1,459 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 390 lines.
* **Status partial:** the engine isn't wired. `syncNow()` answers `unavailable` until M6's `upsertFromSync` and M7's writer are on `develop`. A last small layer will pass them to the engine; then HU-010 and HU-011 work end to end.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No engine until M6 and M7 can write.** Syncing without them would call the bank and throw the data away, against the API limits. M6 and M7 can already call `syncNow()` from pull to refresh.
* **No toast.** The M5 prompt asks for "a small toast", but there's no shared `Toast` in `@shared/ui`, and the design README says to export the toast open before building one (M0, Sofia). `useSync().lastReport` has the result, ready for when it exists.
* **"Reconecta <Entidad>" goes on M6's account card.** M5 marks the link revoked and calls `markLinkRevoked`; M6 draws the card.

When M6's stack (#164 → #167) merges and M7 has its writer: a small layer that passes them to the engine in `index.ts`. Then run Gate B on staging with Banco Demo (needs M16's authorization page and M14's settings).

#### 2026-10-30 · The KMS client assertion signer and token fingerprint

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-kms-keys. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer asked to start step D: "Start with D, but for this part, use a different stack if possible so that we don't have a stack of more than 10 PRs". First layer of a new stack from `develop`: the KMS signer and fingerprint behind the interfaces M5 already has.

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/keys/kms.go`:
  * a small `KMS` interface (`SignDigest`, `GenerateMac`);
  * `KMSSigner`: builds the same claims as the local signer, sends KMS only the SHA-256 digest of `header.payload` (RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256), and appends the signature;
  * `KMSFingerprinter`: KMS `GenerateMac` with an HMAC_256 key; its errors never contain the token.
* `signer.go`: the claims (iss, sub, aud, random jti, exp 60 s) move to `assertionClaims`, shared by both signers. `LocalSigner` behaves the same.
* `kms_test.go`: a fake KMS with local keys (PSS with the salt equal to the hash, as KMS does, and HMAC-SHA-256). It checks:
  * the assertion verifies as PS256 with the public key of an RSA 3072 key (M14's spec);
  * KMS gets only the digest of the signing input, with the right key id;
  * every jti differs;
  * missing values are refused and KMS errors come through;
  * the fingerprint equals HMAC-SHA-256 of the token;
  * no error contains the token.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./internal/bank/...` passes. gofmt, go vet and golangci-lint (0 issues) pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 260 lines.
* Not used yet: the AWS SDK adapter and the settings come in the next layers, and they need the developer's OK (`go.mod` and `platform/config` are M0's).

##### Decisions and problems

* **A new stack,** as the developer asked, so stack #188 stays at 9 PRs.
* **No `kid` header,** as with the local signer. If Banco Demo or a real bank needs one to find Fynk's key, it's one line in `assertionClaims`' caller.
* **Switching to KMS changes every fingerprint,** because the KMS HMAC key isn't the local file key. Links made with the local key won't refresh under KMS. Staging has no links yet (no bank settings), so nothing breaks there.

##### Next step

Ask the developer to approve `chore/add-aws-kms-sdk` (`go.mod`) and the KMS settings in `platform/config`, both reviewed by Sofia.

#### 2026-10-30 · Use the KMS keys for the bank link in AWS

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-kms-wiring. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Last layer of the KMS stack, approved by the developer: "Yes, do it and create the stack with the PRs, Sofia and Juan Pablo must be called to review either way". The SDK adapter, the KMS key settings in `platform/config` (M0, Sofia reviews) and the choice of key in the bank wiring.

##### What was done

* `internal/platform/config` (M0): two optional settings, `BANK_SIGNER_KMS_KEY_ID` and `BANK_FINGERPRINT_KMS_KEY_ID` (id, ARN or alias). Nothing is required, so every environment keeps starting. One test reads them and the "link off" test checks they default to empty.
* `internal/bank/keys/kmsclient.go`: `AWSKMS` over the SDK, `Sign` with `MessageType DIGEST` and `RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256`, and `GenerateMac` with `HMAC_SHA_256`. An empty answer is an error.
* `internal/bank/wiring.go`: `bankKeys` takes each key from KMS when its id is set, else from its file. The AWS client is built once, from the default chain (`AWS_REGION`, the ECS task role). With neither source, or if the AWS client can't load, the link stays off and the revoker stays unavailable, as before.
* Tests:
  * the adapter against a fake KMS endpoint: checks the `X-Amz-Target` operation and the JSON fields (key id, `DIGEST`, `RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256`, `HMAC_SHA_256`, base64 message), errors, and empty answers;
  * the wiring: KMS ids alone build the providers with the KMS fingerprinter and one client; local files never load AWS; an AWS client that can't load keeps the link off.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./...` in `services/api` passes. gofmt, go vet and golangci-lint (0 issues) pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 300 lines.
* Never run against a real KMS: that needs the staging keys and the task role (M14).

##### Decisions and problems

* **Each key chooses on its own,** so staging can move one key at a time. In practice both are set together.
* **No region setting of our own:** `AWS_REGION` is set in ECS. M11 has its own region variable for Bedrock; the KMS keys live in the API's region.
* **For Juan Pablo (M14):**
  * the ECS task needs `BANK_SIGNER_KMS_KEY_ID` and `BANK_FINGERPRINT_KMS_KEY_ID` from the KMS module's outputs;
  * the task role needs `kms:Sign` on the signer key and `kms:GenerateMac` on the fingerprint key;
  * Banco Demo needs the public key of the KMS signer (`kms:GetPublicKey`) to verify the assertions.
* **`.env.example` is unchanged:** local development keeps the key files.

Push the stack and the mTLS test, with Sofia and Juan Pablo as reviewers.

* The certificate cannot be made by Fynk: only Bancolombia can issue one from its test CA. A support request was drafted asking for the certificate, the Open Finance products and a way to attach the certificate.
* The `https://` return link (finding 9) needs a domain and changes in M0's `linking.ts`; left to the team.

#### 2026-11-05 · Align the X-Request-Nonce length with the contract

* Pull request: not opened yet.

* The code follows the contract (contract first), not the other way round: no contract change, nothing to regenerate, and the phone already sends 32 characters.
* The contract marks the header `required: false` while the service always requires it and answers `400 nonce_required`. Left as is: if it were required, the generated handler would refuse a missing header with its own error instead of `nonce_required`.

Mark consents still pending after 24 hours as failed (M4).

#### 2026-10-29 · Accounts in the vault, the write API for M5 and the read API

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-26, SCRUM-27, SCRUM-28. Branch: feature/accounts-vault-and-sync-api. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second part of the first M6 work plan step: "tables, write API for M5". Same session as `2026-10-29-sofia-ariza-783-m6-net-worth.md`, split in its own stacked branch to keep each pull request small.

##### What was done

* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts`: `accounts_accounts` with every `Account` field, a unique index on `(provider, provider_account_id)` (no duplicates), a unique index that allows a single cash account, and `accounts_balance_history (account_id, on_date, balance_minor)`.
* `adapters/vault/vaultAccountsStore.ts`: upsert by `(provider, provider_account_id)` that keeps the first id, so an account linked again keeps its history; one balance point per account per day in Bogotá, the last sync of the day wins.
* `application/accountsService.ts`: `upsertFromSync`, `markLinkRevoked`, `getOverview`, `getAccounts`, `getBalanceTotal`, `getConnectedAccountsSummary`, `getCdtAccounts`, `getBalanceHistory`, and a change listener.
* `application/useAccounts.ts`: TanStack Query under `['accounts']`, refetched only when the service reports a change. While the vault is locked it reads as empty.
* `index.ts`: registers the vault migrations, listens to `sync.completed`, `vault.unlocked` and `vault.locked`, and exports the write and read API. The Dashboard tab is still M0's placeholder.
* 14 tests on a real SQLite vault (sql.js) and on the hook.

##### Result

* Tests: 45 of 45 in the module, 1087 of 1087 in the app. Lint and typecheck clean.
* Pull request: second in the M6 stack.

##### Decisions and problems

* A whole sync is validated before anything is written, so a malformed account never leaves half a sync in the vault.
* Revoked accounts stay in the vault with status `revoked` and leave every list and total. A new link to the same bank brings them back with the same id.
* The query has no time based staleness: data changes only through sync, unlock, lock or a write, which all notify the hook (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

##### Next step

Cash account, its history, the `accounts.cash` backup collection and `CashInput`.

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-26, SCRUM-28, SCRUM-33. Branch: feature/accounts-model-and-net-worth. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia pasted the full M6 prompt ("You are building module M6 of Fynk: the account model on the phone, balances, consolidated net worth, cards, digital wallets and the manual cash account") and asked to follow its work plan. This branch is the first step: "net worth function with tests".

##### What was done

* `domain/netWorth.ts`: `balanceTotal` (savings, checking, wallets and cash, minus what is owed on active credit cards, CDTs left out), `connectedAccountsSummary` ("N cuentas conectadas": active bank accounts, cards and wallets, without cash and CDTs) and `sortAccounts` (accounts, wallets, cards, CDTs, cash).

* `domain/syncedAccounts.ts`: `cleanSyncedAccounts` checks what M5 writes after a sync (no manual or cash accounts, provider account id and link required, integer centavos, CDT details) and keeps one account per `(provider, providerAccountId)` with only the last 4 digits. `bogotaDate` gives the day of a balance history point.

##### Decisions and problems

* The first work plan branch was about 990 lines with tests, so it was split in two stacked branches: this pure domain branch and `feature/accounts-vault-and-sync-api` with the tables, the M5 write API and the read API.
* Credit cards count in "N cuentas conectadas": the prompt excludes only cash and CDTs, and a card is a connected bank product.
* Credit card debt follows the shared contract: `balanceMinor` of a card is the positive amount owed and is subtracted. The demo fixture of M16 has the card balance as a negative number; M5 must map it to the contract.

#### 2026-11-05 · Manual movements change cash and disconnected accounts (M6 side)

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-71. Branch: feature/manual-movement-balances. PR: #264. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia brought a team decision: "each manual movement reduces cash balance. If the bank accounts are not connected, also modify the bank account balance. If the accounts are connected so do not change manually the balance." In plan mode she chose these details:
* "Not connected" means accounts whose link was revoked or closed.
* A Gasto subtracts and an Ingreso adds.
* Only movements recorded in Efectivo change cash.
* A Gasto bigger than the cash is blocked with a message.

The work is split into two stacked branches. This one is M6. The M7 sheet and service come in `feature/manual-movements-update-balances`.

##### What was done

* `domain/manualBalance.ts` (new, pure):
  * `balanceOwner` tells who keeps a balance: cash, a disconnected account, or nobody.
  * `netDeltas` takes the old effect back and applies the new one.
  * `manualBalanceUpdates` gives the new balance, and the available amount for cards, where a Gasto raises the debt.
  * `InsufficientCashError { cashMinor }`.
* `application/manualBalanceService.ts` (new): `applyManualMovement` checks every account, then writes disconnected balances in one transaction (`AccountsStore.setManualBalances`) and cash through the existing `setCash`, which writes the history entry and the backup mark.
* `adapters/vault/vaultAccountsStore.ts`: `setManualBalances` only updates rows that are not active and not manual.
* `AccountsOverview.disconnectedAccounts`: revoked or closed bank accounts, sorted, no CDTs, out of the total and the count.
* `index.ts` exports `applyManualMovement`, `InsufficientCashError` and the `ManualEffect` and `ManualMovementChange` types.
* `docs/modules/M06-accounts.md`: the new rule under Rules.
* Tests written first:
  * `manualBalance.test.ts`, 14 tests.
  * `manualBalanceService.test.ts`, 8 tests on SQLite: cash goes down and so does the total; history and backup mark; delete gives the amount back; nothing written when cash is short; a disconnected account changes and stays out of the total; a connected one never changes; a sync after reconnecting takes the bank balance; the overview lists disconnected accounts.
  * One more case in `index.test.ts`: a manual movement in Efectivo is marked for the backup.

##### Result

* Tests: full mobile suite 212 suites, 2463 tests pass. `tsc --noEmit`, ESLint and Prettier pass.
* Pull request: #264, in stack #263 (#261 → #262 → #264 → #265). The stack was rebased on `develop` b77cfb8 (#258, Go only) before pushing.

##### Decisions and problems

* Cash is written through `setCash`, so each movement adds a cash history entry and raises the backup version, just like typing the amount.
* The error carries the cash there is now, for the message "Tu efectivo es $X".
* Disconnected bank balances are not backed up (only `accounts.cash` is), and those accounts are not shown on the Dashboard or in Mis cuentas, so their new balance is only visible in M7's sheet.

##### Next step

`feature/manual-movements-update-balances` (M7): call `applyManualMovement` on add, edit and delete; offer disconnected accounts in the sheet; the "Tu efectivo es $X" error and the new note.

#### 2026-11-03 · Default categories for synced movements

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-32. Branch: feature/transaction-categorization. PR: #237. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the M7 stack. Work plan step 4, split in three (pipeline, changes, detail screen). HU-018: "Categorías predeterminadas". Test: "Categorization table tests in Spanish, at least 40 merchant strings from the demo fixture".

##### What was done

* `domain/categorize.ts`: the pipeline, first match wins:
  1. The person's category for the bank movement.
  2. The person's category for the merchant.
  3. M9's catalog (optional).
  4. Keyword rules.
  5. Food under the Antojos threshold becomes `treats`.
  6. `other`.
* `domain/rules.es-CO.ts`: ordered keyword rules for Colombian merchants and bank descriptions (Éxito, D1, Ara, Uber, TransMilenio, Netflix, EPM, Claro, nómina, arriendo, droguerías, cine, gimnasio and more). They match whole words on text without accents or case.
* `application/transactionsService.ts`: `categorizeNew(ids)`.
  * Skips movements whose category the person picked.
  * Reads the overrides in one query each.
  * Writes only what changed and emits `transactions.changed`.
* Store: `getMany`, `overridesFor`, `setCategories`.
* `index.ts`: runs `categorizeNew` on `sync.completed` for the new ids, with `getTreatsThreshold()` from M10.
* Tests:
  * Table of 59 strings: the 21 distinct strings of `fixtures/demo-user.json` with the fixture's category, plus 38 Colombian merchants.
  * Pipeline order, the threshold, salary only for money in.
  * `categorizeNew` on the vault: new ids, threshold, the person's category kept, overrides by bank id and by merchant.

##### Result

* Tests: 2292 of 2292 in the app. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, above the search.

##### Decisions and problems

* The prompt asks for 40 merchant strings "from the demo fixture", but the fixture has only 21 distinct ones. The test uses all 21 and adds 38 common Colombian ones.
* Rule text is built by M7 (lowercase, no accents, signs as spaces), not with the shared `normalizeMerchant`, because that one drops digits and "D1" would become "d".
* "Food" for Antojos is Restaurantes and Mercado. In the fixture every café is under $20.000 and every restaurant and supermarket above it, so the fixture categories come out the same.
* `getCatalogCategory` of M9 is not on develop: step 3 is an optional dependency, marked `TODO(M9)` in `index.ts`.
* The categorization does not send any movement text off the phone, and there is no machine learning (the prompt says to ask before either).

##### Next step

`feature/transaction-category-changes`: changing a category, "Aplicar a todos", the history, the overrides backup and the M10 integration test.

#### 2026-11-03 · Changing a category: history, overrides and the M10 totals

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-32. Branch: feature/transaction-category-changes. PR: #238. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the M7 stack. HU-018: "Modificación manual. Historial de cambios. Actualiza reportes asociados". The prompt: "Changing a category asks ¿Aplicar a todos los movimientos de <comercio>? Yes creates an override and recategorizes past movements of that merchant. Each change is stored in transactions_category_changes, then emits transactions.changed." Test: "A recategorization emits transactions.changed and M10's monthly category totals change accordingly (integration test with M10 stub)".

##### What was done

* `application/categoryService.ts`: `changeCategory(id, categoryId, { applyToMerchant })` and `getCategoryHistory(id)`.
  * With "aplicar a todos", every movement of the merchant changes and a merchant override stays for the next ones.
  * Without it, a bank movement keeps an override by the bank's id.
  * One history line per movement whose category really changed, then `transactions.changed`.
* `adapters/vault/vaultCategoryStore.ts`: the change and its history in one vault transaction, and the overrides with their backup version.
* Backup collection `transactions.category_overrides`, records `merchant:<key>` and `transaction:<bank id>`. The highest version wins, so an old backup never undoes a newer choice. It is registered in `index.ts`, and `markDirty` runs after each change.
* `index.ts` exports `changeCategory`, `getCategoryHistory`, `CategoryChange` and `InvalidCategoryError`.
* `src/app/AppRoot.test.tsx` (M0): the vault stand-in gets `registerBackupCollection` and `markDirty`, because M7 is the first module that registers a collection when it loads.
* Tests:
  * One movement; the whole merchant plus the next sync.
  * The history newest first; no history when nothing changes; unknown movement or category.
  * Backup export, restore on a new phone and first sync there, and an old backup that loses.
  * The M10 stub: Antojos goes down and Restaurantes goes up after a change.

##### Result

* Tests: 2300 of 2300 in the app. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, above the categorization.

##### Decisions and problems

* "Aplicar a todos" also changes movements whose category the person had picked one by one: the person asked for all of them.
* The history of bank movements is not in the backup. On a new phone the categories come back through the overrides, but the history lines do not. Backing it up would need a new collection that is not in the shared contracts.
* A manual movement leaves no override; it is backed up whole with `transactions.manual` (next branches).
* The M10 test uses a stub that recounts on `transactions.changed`, because M10's own wiring (`configureAnalytics`) is not called by the app yet.

##### Next step

`feature/transaction-detail-screen`: the movement detail with "Cambiar categoría", the question "¿Aplicar a todos…?" and the history.

#### 2026-11-03 · Filters of Movimientos: the model and its SQL

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-30. Branch: feature/transaction-filters. PR: #234. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the M7 stack. Work plan step 2, split in two: this branch is the filter model and its queries, the next one is the Filtros sheet. HU-016: "Filtros por fecha, monto, cuenta y categoría".

##### What was done

* `domain/query.ts`: `TransactionFilter` gains `kind` (Ingresos or Gastos), `minAmountMinor` and `maxAmountMinor` (without sign, both included) and `categoryIds`.
* `adapters/vault/vaultTransactionsStore.ts`: the WHERE clause for them, shared by the keyset pages, the full lists and the summary.
* `domain/filter.ts`:
  * `presetRange` for "Este mes", "Mes anterior" and "Últimos 3 meses" on Bogotá days, and `presetOf`.
  * `filterProblems`: a range that ends before it starts, negative or broken amounts, a minimum above the maximum.
  * `activeFilterCount`, for the badge on "Filtros".
  * `filterFromParams`, for the `{ categoryId, from, to }` that Estadísticas (M10) already sends and an `{ accountId }`.
* Tests: presets at month and year edges and on the Bogotá day; each filter alone and all combined on the node:sqlite vault, with the summary following the filter.

##### Result

* Tests: 50 of 50 in the module. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, above the preview.

##### Decisions and problems

* "Últimos 3 meses" is the 3 months that end today (23 jun to 22 sep), not 3 calendar months.
* Amount filters compare the amount without sign, so "más de $100.000" finds both a big income and a big expense. "Ingresos" and "Gastos" pick the sign.
* Todos, Ingresos and Gastos live in the filter (and later in the sheet), not as chips over the list, because the design has no chips.

#### 2026-11-03 · Manual movements and the transactions.manual backup

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-29, SCRUM-32. Branch: feature/manual-transactions. PR: #240. Status: done.

Same session as the M7 stack. Work plan step 5: "create, edit, delete, backup collection". The prompt's Nuevo movimiento manual: "amount, ingreso or gasto, account (including Efectivo), date, description, category". This branch is the data side; the sheet comes next.

##### What was done

* `domain/manual.ts`: `ManualInput`, its checks (account, amount in whole centavos above zero, date, description up to 80 characters, category) and the movement to save. Money out is saved negative. The category comes from the person or from the pipeline on the description.
* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts` v3: `transactions_manual_tombstones`, so a deletion reaches other phones through the backup.
* `adapters/vault/vaultManualStore.ts`: save with the backup version up by one, delete with a tombstone, export and import (highest version wins).
* `application/manualService.ts`: `addManualTransaction`, `updateManualTransaction`, `deleteManualTransaction` (manual movements only), each with `markDirty` and `transactions.changed`, and the `transactions.manual` collection.
* A goal (M8) or category change on a manual movement raises its backup version in SQL, and `index.ts` marks it for the backup on `transactions.changed`.
* Tests:
  * Cash expense categorized from its description; income with a picked category; found by the search.
  * Validation; editing keeps the goal; a bank movement can never be edited or deleted; deleting; a sync never removes a manual movement.
  * Backup to a new phone with a deletion; an old backup that loses; a goal change as a new version.

##### Result

* Tests: 2316 of 2316 in the app. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, above the detail screen.

##### Decisions and problems

* A manual movement is a record only. It never changes the balance of a bank account or of Efectivo (rule 6, Fynk never moves money; cash is the amount the person types in M6). This needs the team's review.
* Manual movements have no merchant, so "Aplicar a todos" is never offered for them; the person picks the category of each one.
* Deletions need tombstones because the backup contract sends `deleted: true` records.

#### 2026-11-03 · Search of Movimientos with FTS5

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-31. Branch: feature/transaction-search-fts. PR: #236. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the M7 stack. Work plan step 3: "FTS5 index, triggers to keep it in sync, search bar". HU-017: "Coincidencias parciales, respuesta rápida, sin distinguir mayúsculas y minúsculas, resultados relevantes". Test: "SUPERMERC" and "supermércado" both find "Supermercado".

##### What was done

* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts` v2:
  * `transactions_fts` (FTS5, `content=` the movements table, `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`).
  * Insert, update and delete triggers.
  * A `rebuild` for movements saved before the index.
* `domain/searchQuery.ts`: each typed word becomes a quoted prefix (`"super"*`), so FTS5 syntax typed by the person is plain text; at most 8 words.
* The search joins the filters (`t.rowid IN (SELECT rowid FROM transactions_fts WHERE ... MATCH ?)`) on the keyset pages and the summary.
* `searchTransactions(text)` in `index.ts`: at most 50, newest first.
* Movimientos: "Buscar por nombre" (shared `SearchField`) next to "Filtros". It runs 250 ms after the person stops typing and shows "No encontramos movimientos" when nothing matches.
* Tests:
  * "SUPERMERC" and "supermércado" find "Supermercado".
  * Partial words in description or merchant; every word needed; syntax as text.
  * Search plus filters; the index follows updates and deletes; old rows indexed by the rebuild.
  * 3.000 movements answer in under 300 ms.
  * The screen searches as the person types.

##### Result

* Tests: 2221 of 2221 in the app. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, above the Filtros sheet.

##### Decisions and problems

* Results are ordered newest first, like the rest of the list. Every word must match, so results stay relevant without a rank.
* The phone's SQLCipher is built with FTS5 (`"op-sqlite": { "fts5": true }`); the tests run on node:sqlite, which has FTS5 too.
* An external content index refers to `rowid`. If M2 ever runs `VACUUM` on the vault, `INSERT INTO transactions_fts (transactions_fts) VALUES ('rebuild')` has to run after it.
* A debounced search in Jest needs the debounce timer to run inside `act`; outside it, React kept the update until the end of the test. The test helper waits inside `act`.
* Gate C asks for under 300 ms on a mid range Android phone. The Jest check runs on a laptop; the phone check stays for Sofia once sync brings 12 months of data.

##### Next step

`feature/transaction-categorization`: the categorization pipeline and its rules.

#### 2026-11-03 · A node:sqlite test vault for M7, bottom of the M7 stack

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-31. Branch: test/transactions-sqlite-test-vault. PR: #229. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia pasted the full M7 prompt (movement list, filters, search, categories, manual movements) and chose, after planning: "Whole module as a stack", "Follow the design" and build the missing sheets "from components". This branch is the first piece of that stack.

##### What was done

* `apps/mobile/src/modules/transactions/testing/nodeSqliteVault.ts`: a `VaultDb` for tests on Node's own SQLite (`node:sqlite`), with the same migration rules as the shared test vault (table prefix per module, versions tracked per module, one transaction at a time).
* Three tests: FTS5 with `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2` finds "Café Señor" with `"senor"*`, a failed transaction rolls back, and a table without the `transactions_` prefix is refused.

##### Result

* Tests: 3 of 3. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean on the module.
* Pull request: in the M7 stack, bottom.

##### Decisions and problems

* The shared test vault uses sql.js, which has FTS3 and FTS4 but not FTS5 (`no such module: fts5`). The phone (op-sqlite with SQLCipher) has FTS5 only. So the search index of M7 can only be tested on an SQLite that has FTS5. Node 22.20 (CI and local) has it, with accent folding.
* Jest does not know `node:sqlite` as a module, so the file loads it with `process.getBuiltinModule`.
* It stays inside M7. Moving it to `shared/testing` for other modules is an M0 follow-up.

##### Next step

`feature/transactions-store-and-pagination`: the vault tables and the store on top of this vault.

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-29. Branch: feature/transactions-store-and-pagination. PR: #230. Status: done.

Same session as `test/transactions-sqlite-test-vault`: the M7 prompt, built as one stack. This branch is work plan step 1 without the screens: "tables, keyset pagination".

##### What was done

* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts` (v1): `transactions_transactions` as in the prompt, plus `backup_version` for manual rows, the category change history, the merchant overrides and a table of overrides for single bank movements (by `providerTxId`, so a new phone applies them again after its first sync).
* `adapters/vault/vaultTransactionsStore.ts`: upsert by `(account_id, provider_tx_id)` that keeps the category and the goal, deletes pending movements the bank no longer returns, keyset pages on `(booked_at, id)` and lists by UTC range.
* `domain/bogota.ts` (Bogotá days to UTC bounds), `domain/query.ts` (filter, cursor, pages of 20), `domain/syncedTransactions.ts` (checks what M5 hands over).

##### Result

* A movement synced again keeps the id it had first, its category and its goal: the bank owns what happened, the person owns the category.
* `booked_at` is stored as UTC with milliseconds, so text order is time order and the keyset compare is right.
* The PR is about 850 lines, roughly 40 % tests. The store and its tests are kept together.
* `getTransactionsBetween` takes Bogotá days, both included, as M10's `listTransactions` port expects.

#### 2026-11-05 · Categorize with the M9 merchant catalog

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-32, SCRUM-31. Branch: feature/transaction-catalog-category. PR: #272. Status: partial.

##### Instruction

Close two items left open in earlier M7 session logs: "Hook up catalogCategory: getCatalogCategory once M9 exports it" and "Gate C: search over 12 months of data in under 300 ms on a mid-range Android phone", in one pull request if that is correct.

##### What was done

* At first `develop` had no `getCatalogCategory`. The plan was to leave M7 ready and ask Karol. During the session, Karol pushed `feature/subscription-cancellation-guide` (SCRUM-58), which exports `getCatalogCategory(merchantKey): string | null`. Sofia chose to stack this branch on it.
* `adapters/catalog/catalogCategory.ts`: turns M9's lookup into the catalog step of the categorization. It keeps only category ids the app knows (`isCategoryId`), and a lookup that throws counts as no category.
* `index.ts`: `catalogCategory` wired to M9 and loaded on first use (inline `require`, like `vault/adapters/crypto/argon2Kdf.ts`).
* Tests:
  * `catalogCategory.test.ts`: known category, unknown merchant, a category the app does not know, a failing lookup.
  * `categorization.test.ts`: `categorizeNew` with the catalog. The catalog beats the keyword rules, the person's merchant category beats the catalog, small food stays `treats`, and the catalog is asked only for movements with a merchant key.

##### Result

* Tests: 2448 of 2448 in the app. Lint, typecheck and Prettier clean on the touched files.
* Removing the catalog step from `categorize.ts` makes the new service test fail.
* Pull request: #272, on top of Karol's branch, which has no pull request yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Import cycle.** M9 imports M7 and reads `getTransactionsBetween`, `getAccounts` and `TRANSACTION_DETAIL_ROUTE` while its file loads. The registry loads accounts, then transactions. A top-level import of M9 in M7 would load M9 halfway through M7, and the app crashes on start (checked: `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'DETAIL_ROUTE')`). So M7 loads M9 on first use. If M9 ever reads those exports only inside functions, a normal import works.
* M9 returns `string`, not `CategoryId`, because the catalog comes from the backend. M7 checks the id instead of trusting it.
* Until M9 has read its catalog from the vault (`vault.unlocked`), every lookup is null and the keyword rules decide. The first sync after opening the app can run before that. Those movements keep the rule's category, and the person can still change it.
* Gate C (SCRUM-31), the timing on a mid-range Android phone, was skipped by decision. No phone was reachable from this machine. The Jest check (3.000 movements under 300 ms on a laptop) still passes. The phone check stays open.
* `git fetch` failed with an SSL certificate error. It worked with `-c http.sslBackend=schannel`, which still checks certificates.

##### Next step

* Karol opens the pull request of `feature/subscription-cancellation-guide`. Then this pull request is linked into a stack (`gh stack link`) and merges after it.
* Gate C: time the search on a mid-range Android phone with 12 months of data.

#### 2026-11-05 · Manual movements change cash and disconnected accounts (M7 side)

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-29, SCRUM-32. Branch: feature/manual-movements-update-balances. PR: #265. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The same team decision as the M6 log of today (`2026-11-05-sofia-ariza-783-m6-manual-movement-balances.md`): "each manual movement reduces cash balance. If the bank accounts are not connected, also modify the bank account balance. If the accounts are connected so do not change manually the balance." This branch makes M7's manual movements use M6's `applyManualMovement`, stacked on the M6 branch.

##### What was done

* `application/ports.ts`: `ManualBalancePort` and `ManualBalanceChange` (before and after, with signed amounts).
* `application/manualService.ts`: add, edit and delete send the change to M6 first. If M6 refuses (not enough cash), nothing is saved, marked or announced. If the save or delete fails after M6 applied the change, the opposite change gives the balance back. Delete now reads the movement first, so a bank movement still gets `TransactionNotFoundError` and never touches a balance. Restores do not call M6.
* `index.ts`: passes `applyManualMovement`, and gives the sheet `cashShortOf` built on M6's `InsufficientCashError`.
* `ui/ManualTransactionSheet.tsx`:
  * Lists disconnected accounts after the active ones, with "· Desconectada".
  * Shows "Tu efectivo es $X" under Monto when cash is short.
  * The note follows the account picked (Efectivo, disconnected, connected).
* `ui/strings.es-CO.ts`: the new copy. It replaces "Es un registro: no mueve dinero ni cambia el saldo de tus cuentas." It needs design review.
* `testing/renderScreen.tsx`: a no-op balance port for the screen tests.
* `docs/modules/M07-transactions.md`: the new rule under Screens.
* Tests written first:
  * `manualService.test.ts`, 7 new: Gasto and Ingreso signs; edit with an account change; delete; nothing saved when M6 refuses an add, an edit or a delete; the balance given back when the save fails; no balance change for a bank movement or a restore.
  * `ManualTransactionSheet.test.tsx`, 3 new: the disconnected chip; the note for each kind of account; "Tu efectivo es $30.000", with the sheet staying open.

##### Result

* Tests: full mobile suite 212 suites, 2473 tests pass. `tsc --noEmit`, ESLint and Prettier pass.
* Pull request: #265, top of stack #263 (#261 → #262 → #264 → #265).
* Not checked on a phone yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* The balance changes before the movement is saved, so a refused Gasto never leaves a movement without its balance. Undoing it after a failed save is done in code, not in one database transaction, because the two writes belong to two modules.
* The sheet tests first expected "Banco Demo · Ahorros". The label uses the type name, "Banco Demo · Cuenta de ahorros", so the tests were corrected.
* Not done: when M6 refuses a delete (for example, deleting an Ingreso in cash that was already spent), Detalle de movimiento closes the alert and shows nothing. That was already true for any failed delete. It needs a message and copy.
* `CLAUDE.md` › Decisions taken is not updated yet: it is a shared M0 file, so Sofia decides first.

##### Next step

Review of the stack #261 → #262 → M6 → M7. Then the message in Detalle de movimiento when a delete is refused, and the `CLAUDE.md` decision row.

#### 2026-11-07 · Keep the account name of movements after a bank link is revoked

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-29. Branch: fix/transaction-account-after-revoke. PR: #331. Status: done.

##### Instruction

From the follow-ups of the M4 and M5 end-to-end test: "After revoking a bank connection, the account name disappears from the transactions on the Dashboard. Before revocation, it displays 'Savings account · Oct 26,' but afterward, it shows only 'Oct 26.'"

##### What was done

* Cause: M6's `markLinkRevoked` moves the link's accounts from `accounts` to `disconnectedAccounts`, and M7 looked accounts up only in `accounts`. The same bug removed the entity from Movimientos ("Salario · Bancolombia" → "Salario") and showed "—" as the account in the movement detail.
* `transactions/ui/labels.ts`: `knownAccounts(data)` joins `accounts` and `disconnectedAccounts`, and an `AccountsData` type for M6's data.
* `TransactionPreview.tsx`, `AllTransactionsScreen.tsx`, `TransactionDetailScreen.tsx` use it. FiltersSheet still lists active accounts only.
* Tests: a movement whose account is only in `disconnectedAccounts` keeps "Cuenta de ahorros · 26 oct" (Dashboard), "Salario · Bancolombia" (Movimientos) and "Bancolombia · Cuenta de ahorros" (detail). There is also a unit test for `knownAccounts`.

##### Result

* Tests: 351 of 351 pass in `transactions` and `accounts`. ESLint, Prettier and `tsc --noEmit` are clean.
* Pull request: #331.

##### Decisions and problems

* The fix is in M7, which reads the data M6 already gives (`disconnectedAccounts`). No new M6 API was needed.
* CDTs of a revoked link are not in `disconnectedAccounts` (M6 leaves them out). They have no movements in the demo data, so this was left as is.

##### Next step

Check on the emulator: revoke Banco Demo and see that the Dashboard rows keep "Cuenta de ahorros · <fecha>".

#### 2026-10-29 · Savings goals in the vault: create, edit, delete and the suggested monthly amount

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-51. Branch: feature/savings-goal-store. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After SCRUM-56, Claude proposed starting M8 with SCRUM-51 and Karol answered "si". The story is HU-035 Crear meta: "Nombre, categoría, monto objetivo y fecha límite. Aporte mensual sugerido. Varias metas, editar y eliminar. Disponible en el paso 4 y desde el Dashboard. Bóveda cifrada."

##### What was done

The story is split into stacked pull requests. This first one holds the vault and the service, without screens.

* **`savings/domain/goal.ts`:**
  * `validateGoal`: name 1 to 40 characters, target above $0 and up to $1.000.000.000, deadline a real month and not before the current one.
  * `summarizeGoal`: saved amount, percent, remaining, months left and suggested monthly, from the rules in `goals.ts`.
  * `byDeadline`: nearest deadline first, as in "Próximas metas · Por fecha límite".
* **`adapters/vault/migrations.ts`:** `savings_goals` and `savings_contributions`, as in the M8 prompt. A unique index makes the same movement count for one goal at most. The reminders table comes with SCRUM-53.
* **`adapters/vault/vaultGoalStore.ts`:** list, get, save (upsert), remove (the goal and its contributions in one transaction), and the saved amount per goal (the sum of contributions that are not deleted).
* **`application/goalService.ts`:**
  * create, update and remove; editing keeps the id and creation time.
  * list, nearest deadline first, with the numbers of each card.
  * `monthlyTotal`: goals already reached are left out.
  * subscribe.
* **`index.ts`:** registers the migrations and exports `getGoals()` and `getGoalsMonthlyTotal()` for M3. The Metas route is still M0's placeholder until the next pull request.
* **Tests:**
  * `domain/goal.test.ts` (9): form rules, "8 meses · $625.000", 64 %, the Regalos de Navidad card ($140.000 suggested, $280.000 left, 65 %), a reached goal, the order.
  * `application/goalService.test.ts` (13): vault round trip, several goals, saved as the sum of contributions, deleted contributions, edit, a past deadline, delete with contributions, invalid input, subscribers, "$840.000/mes", Bogotá date.

##### Result

* Tests: 81 suites and 1063 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.
* Pull request: pushed. The browser that opens pull requests from Karol's account disconnected during the session, so the pull request still has to be opened.

##### Decisions and problems

* **An old goal can be edited without changing its deadline,** even if that month already passed. A new deadline cannot be in the past.
* **Deleting a goal deletes its contributions.** They are only records, and no other module points to them yet. When SCRUM-52 links movements, the delete also has to clear the `goalId` of each movement.
* **Categories follow the M8 prompt** (Viaje, Fondo de emergencia, Educación, Hogar, Vehículo, Otro). The design's sample data also shows "Celebración" and "Tecnología"; they are not added until the team decides.

##### Next step

The goal form (GoalForm, as in OnbMetas, with the live "Aporte mensual sugerido" box) and the Metas screen, stacked on this branch.

#### 2026-10-29 · Milestone notices at 50 %, 75 % and 100 %

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-53. Branch: feature/savings-milestones. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While #174 (contributions) waited for approval, Karol asked: "revisa que puedes avanzar con lo que esta ya mezclado y ve avanzando". The milestone notices of SCRUM-53 (HU-037 Seguimiento: "Avisos en 50 %, 75 % y 100 %") depend only on what is already in `develop`.

##### What was done

* **`domain/milestones.ts`:** `planMilestones(percent, noticed)`.
  * Records the milestones reached for the first time.
  * Notices only the highest of them: a jump from 40 % to 80 % gives one notice, for 75 %.
* **Migration 2 of `savings`:** table `savings_milestones (goal_id, milestone, reached_at)`. Deleting a goal deletes its milestones too (`vaultGoalStore.remove`).
* **`adapters/vault/vaultMilestoneStore.ts` and `application/milestonePorts.ts`.**
* **`application/milestoneNotifier.ts`:** `check()` notifies through M12's `NotificationsPort`.
  * Kind `goal_milestone`, title the goal's name, link `fynk://metas`, dedupe key `goal:<id>:<milestone>`.
  * Body as in the Notificaciones design ("Llegaste al 64 % de tu meta."), or "Cumpliste tu meta de $5.000.000. ¡Bien hecho!" at 100 %.
  * The notice goes first and the milestone is stored after, so a failure is retried and M12's key never shows it twice.
* **`index.ts`:** checks when the vault opens and after every change of the goals (with #174, also after every contribution). Only with the app open.
* **Tests:**
  * `domain/milestones.test.ts` (5).
  * `application/milestoneNotifier.test.ts` (7): the design's notice, nothing below 50 % and no repeats, 49, 51, 49, 51 notices once (M8 prompt test), one notice for a jump, 100 %, a failing M12, deleting a goal.

##### Result

* Tests: 107 suites and 1301 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.
* Merged locally with #174's branch: no conflicts.
* Pull request: opened from Karol's account against `develop`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The module keeps its own record of the milestones reached,** besides M12's dedupe key. A milestone passed by a jump is stored without a notice and never comes later, even if the saving goes down and up again.
* **The notice shows the real percent** ("Llegaste al 80 %"), as in the design's example (64 %), not the milestone number.
* **"Vas a tiempo / Te faltan $X al mes" is the other half of SCRUM-53.** The rule is in `domain/goals.ts` (`pace`); it goes on the Metas card next.

"Vas a tiempo" on the Metas card, then "Abonar a esta meta" once #174 is merged.

#### 2026-11-03 · Goals and aportes in the encrypted backup (HU-045)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-52, SCRUM-51. Branch: feature/savings-encrypted-backup. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Third item of the list Karol approved ("Si, hazlo"): do the TODO that Daniel (M2) left in `savings/adapters/backup/savingsBackup.ts`, so goals and aportes travel in the encrypted backup to a new phone.

##### What was done

* **Migration 3** (versions 1 and 2 untouched):
  * adds `version` to goals and aportes, and `deleted_at` to goals;
  * adds `savings_backup_pending`, filled with the rows that already existed.
* **Stores:**
  * Every write bumps `version` and calls M2's `markDirty` with the collection and the id.
  * Deleting a goal no longer erases it. It leaves tombstones on the goal and on each of its aportes (the milestones are still deleted), and marks all of them dirty.
  * The readers hide deleted goals.
  * `GoalStore.remove` gets the time from the service clock.
* **`createSavingsBackup`:**
  * `exportAll` covers both collections, deleted rows included.
  * `importAll` runs in one transaction. The highest version wins, tombstones are honoured, and records with an unexpected shape are skipped.
  * An aporte whose goal has not arrived yet waits for the goals, because M2 does not promise an order between collections.
  * An aporte of a deleted goal, or of a movement another aporte already counts, is kept as deleted.
  * After an import, Metas reloads (`notifyChanged`). An import marks nothing dirty.
  * `flushPending` runs on `vault.unlocked`: it marks the old rows dirty once and empties the table.
* **`index.ts`:** registers `savings.goals` and then `savings.contributions` with `registerBackupCollection`.
* **Tests:** `adapters/backup/savingsBackup.test.ts` (8):
  * markDirty on create, edit, aporte and undo;
  * tombstones when a goal is deleted;
  * a round trip in both import orders;
  * an older version does not overwrite;
  * a tombstone deletes;
  * a bad shape is skipped;
  * old rows are marked once.

##### Result

* Tests: 190 suites and 2339 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.
* Milestones are not backed up, as Daniel's TODO says. After a restore, a milestone already noticed may be noticed once more.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The old rows go through a table, not a flag.** A migration can only run SQL, so it lists the rows, and the first unlock marks them dirty.
* **Waiting aportes.** M2 imports collections in download order, and an aporte references its goal (foreign key). So an aporte that arrives first is kept in memory until the goals are imported.
* **The link from a movement is not restored on M7's side.** The aporte keeps `transactionId`, but the "Aporte a plan de ahorro" label of the movement lives in M7. Bank movements are never backed up, and they come back with the next sync.

##### Next step

The same for M9: `recurring.items` and `recurring.usage_marks`.

#### 2026-11-04 · Payday reminder of a goal, part 1: rule, table and service

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-53. Branch: feature/savings-payday-reminder. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Karol said "si" to "Programar" in Recomendaciones. The M8 prompt says: "choose an amount and the salary day (prefilled from M10's recurring income), schedules a monthly M12 reminder 'Hoy es día de pago: aparta $300.000 para Viaje a Cartagena'. It never moves money."

##### What was done

* **Split.** The whole feature was about 850 lines. It is split into two pull requests merged one after the other (no stack). This one has the rule and the service; the screen comes when this one is merged.
* **`domain/payday.ts` (pure):**
  * `validatePayday`: amount above $0, day from 1 to 31.
  * `nextPaydayAt(day, now)`: 9:00 Bogotá time (14:00 UTC). It moves to next month once 9:00 has passed, and falls on the last day of a shorter month.
  * `salaryDay(sources)`: the usual day of the biggest salary M10 found.
* **Migration 4:** `savings_payday_reminders` (one per goal: amount, day).
* **`createVaultPaydayStore`** and **`createPaydayService`**:
  * `save` validates and schedules the next M12 notification. The id is per goal, so editing replaces it; the dedupe key is per goal and month.
  * `remove` cancels it.
  * `refresh` schedules next month again, and drops the reminders of reached or deleted goals.
* **`index.ts`:** `refresh` runs on `vault.unlocked`. Deleting a goal also removes its reminder.
* **Strings:** `paydayStrings`, the screen's copy included.
* **Tests:**
  * `domain/payday.test.ts` (6): validation; the next payday; the last day of a short month; across the year; the Bogotá day; the salary day.
  * `application/paydayService.test.ts` (5): the design text at 9:00; editing replaces; removing cancels; bad input is refused; the vault opening schedules next month and drops a deleted goal.

##### Result

* 205 suites and 2424 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.

##### Decisions and problems

* **One notice per month, scheduled again on open.** M12's `schedule` fires once and there is no background work. So the next month is scheduled whenever the vault opens: if the app is not opened for a whole month, a month is skipped. The same limit applies to M9's reminders.
* **Kind `recommendation`.** It comes from a recommendation, and `NotificationKind` has no payday kind.
* **Not backed up yet.** The reminder is manual data, but it is not in the backup collections of shared contracts 3.2. It can join `savings.goals` later if the team wants.

#### 2026-10-28 · Detection of recurring payments (M9 domain)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-55. Branch: feature/recurring-detection. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After #95 was merged, Karol asked to go on: "listo, sigue". Claude had proposed the detection algorithm as the next M9 step because it is pure and can be tested on the shared fixture.

##### What was done

* `modules/recurring/domain/detect.ts`: `detectRecurring`, steps 1 to 9 of the M9 prompt. It groups booked outflows by `merchantKey` and keeps groups with 3 charges, or 2 when the catalog says the merchant is a subscription. It classifies the rhythm by the median interval: daily on the weekdays seen, weekly on the most common weekday, biweekly, monthly on the most common day of the month, or yearly. Amounts must be within ± 10 % of the median, or ± 35 % for utilities and telecom. The score is 0,45 for regularity, 0,45 for amount stability and 0,1 for a catalog match, and a group is proposed at 0,7 or more. Each proposal has a kind, the amount of the last charge, the next charge, the account or card of the last charge and the movements behind it. A dismissed proposal returns only after an amount change of more than 30 %.
* `detect.test.ts`: 16 tests. On `fixtures/demo-user.json` it finds the 8 seeded recurring payments: rent on day 1, utilities with a variable amount, the gym every Monday, transport Monday to Friday, and 4 subscriptions that add up to $112.600. It flags none of café, restaurants, supermarket, the 3 big purchases, or moves between own accounts. Hand-made cases cover the 2-charge catalog rule, ± 10 % against ± 35 %, the 31st in a 30 day month, the latest amount, a stopped payment, dismissed proposals, biweekly charges and Bogotá calendar days.

##### Result

* Tests: 41 in the module pass (16 new). ESLint, `tsc` and Prettier are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* Transfers, card payments, savings and income are never proposed: they move the person's own money and would count it twice. The M9 prompt does not say this; it follows from what a recurring expense is.
* A payment with no charge for two periods (for example 65 days for a monthly one) is not proposed, so a service the person already cancelled does not come back.
* The catalog is passed as a lookup function. The real one comes from `GET /catalog/merchants` when the catalog backend is built (it needs Go 1.27, not available in this session).

##### Next step

Open the pull request. Then `feature/recurring-items-manual` when M2's vault is in `develop`, or the catalog backend where Go 1.27 is installed.
