##### Result

* Tests: 79 suites and 1042 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.
* Checked that `billetera/recurrentes/<id>` resolves to `recurring.Edit` with the id, and that `billetera/recurrentes/nuevo` still opens the new item form.
* Pull request: stacked on the rescue pull request.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Daily items get no reminder.** A notice every weekday for transport would only be noise. The switch still shows in the form; the team can change this.
* **When the reminder of the next charge already passed** (an item added the day before it is charged), the reminder is for the charge after, so no reminder is ever sent late with the wrong "Vence en 2 días".
* **No background work.** Reminders are rescheduled only with the app open; the phone rings the local notification that was already scheduled.
* **Merging the stack.** It reached the wrong branches because the upper pull requests were merged without first changing their base to `develop`. Next time: merge the bottom one, delete its branch (GitHub then moves the next base to `develop`), click "Update branch", and only then merge.

#### 2026-11-04 · Detection of recurring payments after each sync (SCRUM-55)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-55. Branch: feature/recurring-detection-on-sync. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Karol said "si" to the next step: SCRUM-55, detect recurring payments after each sync, plus the "Detectamos pagos recurrentes" screen. This pull request is the detection; the screen comes in the next one.

##### What was done

* **Review first:**
  * The pure algorithm (`domain/detect.ts`) was already in `develop` since 28 Oct. The local branch `feature/recurring-detection` was stale and was deleted.
  * GitHub: `gh` has an expired token in this session. `git push` and the read-only API work, so PRs are opened with a prefilled link.
* **Migration 3:** `recurring_proposals` (merchant, score, proposal JSON, status pending/dismissed/confirmed, amount, dates).
* **`createVaultProposalStore`:**
  * pending proposals;
  * decisions (dismissed with their amount, and confirmed);
  * `replacePending`, which drops the proposals no longer detected and counts the new ones;
  * `decide`.
* **`createDetectionService`:**
  * `run()` reads M7's outflows of the last 365 days and calls `detectRecurring`. Merchants of existing items and of confirmed proposals are known; dismissed ones keep their amount. Returns how many proposals are new.
  * `confirm(merchantKey, edits)` creates a detected item (name and amount can be edited).
  * `dismiss(merchantKey)`.
* **`recurringService.create`** takes an optional `{ merchantKey }`. With it, the item is saved with `source: 'detected'`.
* **`index.ts`:** runs detection on `sync.completed` and emits `recurring.detected` when there are new proposals. The catalog lookup returns null until SCRUM-58.
* **Tests:** `application/detectionService.test.ts` (12) on a real vault:
  * the 12 month window and the stored proposal;
  * nothing is confirmed by itself;
  * only new ones are counted;
  * a stopped charge goes away;
  * confirming creates the detected item and stops proposing it, even after the item is deleted;
  * the amount can be edited;
  * an unknown proposal is refused;
  * a dismissed one comes back only when the amount changes by more than 30 %.

##### Result

* 196 suites and 2380 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.

##### Decisions and problems

* **A full run, not an incremental one.** Detection runs over all 12 months on every sync, instead of only the groups with new movements as the prompt says. It is a pure pass over one year of movements and keeps the code simple. It can become incremental if it shows up as slow.
* **A deleted detected item is not proposed again,** because its proposal stays confirmed.
* **Proposals are not backed up.** They are recomputed from the movements. After a restore, a dismissed proposal can show up once more.

##### Next step

The "Detectamos pagos recurrentes" screen with confirm and dismiss per item, opened on `recurring.detected`.

#### 2026-11-04 · Fixed expenses and subscriptions in the encrypted backup (HU-045)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-56, SCRUM-57. Branch: feature/recurring-encrypted-backup. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

"revisa y sigue con lo que toque".

* **Review:** #246 (data for M10) and #247 (savings backup) were already merged. `feature/savings-link-movement` had a conflict in the imports of `savings/index.ts`. It was rebased on `develop` and pushed with `--force-with-lease`; 2374 tests pass.
* **Next:** the same backup for M9.

##### What was done

* **Migration 2** (version 1 untouched): `version` and `deleted_at` on `recurring_items`, and `recurring_backup_pending`, filled with the items that already existed.
* **Store:**
  * Every write bumps `version` and calls M2's `markDirty('recurring.items', id)`: create, edit, usage, reminder, "No es una suscripción" and "Ya la cancelé".
  * Deleting leaves a tombstone, and the readers hide it.
  * `RecurringStore.remove` gets the time from the service clock.
* **`adapters/backup/recurringBackup.ts`:**
  * `exportAll` includes tombstones.
  * `importAll` runs in one transaction: the highest version wins, tombstones are honoured, and records with an unexpected category, schedule or shape are skipped.
  * After a restore, Billetera reloads (new `notifyChanged` on the service) and the reminders are planned again.
  * `flushPending` runs on `vault.unlocked`.
* **`index.ts`:** registers `recurring.items`.
* **Tests:** `adapters/backup/recurringBackup.test.ts` (7):
  * every write marks the item;
  * deleting leaves a tombstone;
  * a round trip keeps the schedule and the usage mark, and marks nothing;
  * an older version does not overwrite;
  * a tombstone deletes;
  * bad data is skipped;
  * old items are marked once.

##### Result

* 195 suites and 2370 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean. The tsc error in `recommendations` was only old generated types in the local copy; `make generate-contract generate-ts` fixed it.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No separate `recurring.usage_marks` collection.** The usage mark is a column of `recurring_items`, not a table of its own, so it travels inside each item. Shared contracts 3.2 lists `recurring.usage_marks`. If the team wants the usage history (several marks per item), it would be a table and a collection of their own.
* **Reminders after a restore.** Local reminders are not backed up. They are planned again on the new phone from the restored items.

##### Next step

SCRUM-55: detect recurring payments in M7's movements after each sync, plus the "Detectamos pagos recurrentes" screen.

  * `GET /catalog/merchants` with `If-None-Match`, `200` with `ETag` and `304` without a body.

  * The example health path is removed.

  * `1_create_merchants` with `cancel_url` restricted to `https://`.
  * `2_seed_merchants` with 47 merchants: streaming, music, cloud, AI, games, gyms, telecom and utilities common in Colombia, plus Banco Demo's fictional ones.
  * `cancel_url` is set only for the 12 whose official page was checked on 5 Nov 2026: Netflix, Spotify, Disney+, HBO Max, YouTube, Apple, Google One, Crunchyroll, Deezer, ChatGPT, Microsoft and PlayStation. The others get general steps.
* **Go:**
  * `merchants.go`: `Load` with an ETag from the hash of the content, and `Matches` (list, weak form and `*`, RFC 9110).
  * `store.go`: pgx.
  * `http.go`: the strict server.
  * `module.go`: behind `auth.Require`, with `Migrations()`.
* **Tests:** `merchants_test.go` (ETag follows the content, If-None-Match) and `http_test.go` (200 with ETag, empty fields as `null` and `[]`, 304 with no body, store error gives 500).
* **`internal/app/app_test.go` (M0, with Karol's approval):** catalog joins the modules with real endpoints in the two tests that expected the stub. This follows what identity, backup, recommendations and market already did.

##### Result

* On Karol's computer: `go vet ./...` is clean and `go test ./internal/catalog/...` passes.
* `internal/identity/keycloak` and `internal/recommendations` could not run there, because Windows Application Control blocked their test executables. That is local only; CI runs on Linux.
* First compile error found this way: the optional `ETag` header is generated as `*string`. Fixed.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Steps as a list, not markdown.** `cancelSteps` is `string[]` instead of the prompt's `cancel_steps_md`, so the app shows numbered steps without a markdown renderer.
* **No URL without checking it.** A wrong cancellation link would send people to the wrong page.
* **The seed migration was not run against Postgres here.** CI's integration tests only migrate `identity` and `backup`; the catalog seed is checked when the API starts with `make dev`.

Julián Castiblanco (JulCR34l) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-60, SCRUM-35, SCRUM-36, SCRUM-37. Branch: feature/spending-caps. PR: none. Status: done.

* Vault tables `analytics_caps`, `analytics_settings`, `analytics_cap_alerts` and the store, backup collection `analytics.caps` (caps and the "Antojos" threshold).
* `analyticsService`: caps CRUD, suggested cap (previous period rounded up to $10.000), alerts through M12 at 80 % and 100 % once per cap, period and level, evaluated on `sync.completed`, `transactions.changed` and `vault.unlocked`.
* Screens (new, need design review): Gastos por categoría with periods, donut and categories, Tendencias (3, 6, 12 months, line chart), Ingresos recurrentes, Topes and the cap form (prefilled from "Fijar tope").

* 58 analytics tests pass, the app shell tests too.

##### Decisions and problems

* M2, M7, M8, M9 and M12 have not exported their functions yet, so the module takes them through `configureAnalytics`. Until it is called, the screens show their empty state. Whoever wires the app (M0, with M2) calls it once at startup.
* A cap that jumps past 100 % sends only "reached" and records the 80 % alert too, so it never arrives late.
* Found in M0's `LineChart`: `EndDot` uses a hook but gifted-charts calls it as a function, so React warns about hook order when the number of points changes. The screen keys the chart to avoid it; M0 should render it as an element.

#### 2026-11-05 · Add the numbers of the Estadísticas screen

Julián Castiblanco (JulCR34l) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-35, SCRUM-36. Branch: feature/analytics-statistics. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Julián asked to keep working on his tasks until they are at 100 % while M14 answers about staging: "sigamos haciendo las demás tareas hasta que queden al 100%".

##### What was done

* Review of the criteria of M10, M11 and M15 against `develop`. Everything is covered by tests, except that the design's Estadísticas screen (screen 14, M10) is only partly built and no button opens it: the design opens it from a chart icon on the Dashboard.
* `domain/statistics.ts` (pure, Bogotá days, integer centavos):
  * `periodSummary`: spending of "3 días", "Semana", "Mes" (last 30 days) or "Año" against the previous period, one cumulative point per day for both, income, balance and today's spending.
  * `periodStats`: daily average, highest expense, movement count and difference with the previous period, saving against the previous period.
  * `weekdayPattern`: average spending of each day of the week and the peak day against Monday to Friday ("Los sábados gastas en promedio el doble que entre semana").
  * `topPlaces`: the day to day places with the most spending. Fixed bills (rent, utilities, internet and phone, subscriptions) are left out, as in the design, because Billetera shows them.
* `analyticsService.getStatistics(period)` reads both periods once; `useStatistics(period)` for the screen.
* Tests on a fixture built from the design's numbers: $3.482.400, $116.080 a day, Balance +$2.097.600, Arriendo $1.400.000 on 1 oct, 64 movements (12 more), $302.800 saved, Supermercado, Recargas de transporte and Restaurantes.

##### Result

* Mobile: tsc, ESLint, Prettier and 70 analytics tests pass.
* Pull request: opened against `develop`.

##### Decisions and problems

* "Ahorraste … frente al mes anterior" is read as what was spent less than in the previous period.
* "Movimientos" counts every movement of the period, money in and out.
* The Banco de la República file could not be checked: its data portal (Suameca) blocks automated downloads. Someone has to download it once by hand.
* Old remote branches could not be deleted from this session (GitHub refuses it); Julián deletes them by hand.

#### 2026-10-28 · POST /recommendations: Bedrock with post checks and fallback

Julián Castiblanco (JulCR34l) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-38, SCRUM-39, SCRUM-40. Branch: feature/recommendations-endpoint. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Julián asked Claude (claude.ai, Fynk project, session linked to the repository zip): "soy la persona 6, que hace la parte de analytics AI and investment, ayúdame a realizarlo con la información que hay en el repo y con respecto al módulo". Claude followed `docs/modules/M10-analytics.md`, `M11-recommendations.md` and `M15-investment.md`, `00-shared-contracts.md` and the design sources, and built the three modules as one stack of branches.

##### What was done

* `contracts/modules/recommendations.yaml`: `POST /recommendations` with the snapshot schema (no extra fields, 12 months, enums), `400 invalid_snapshot`, `429 rate_limited`. The example health path is removed.
* `internal/recommendations/advisor`: `Decode` (16 KB limit, unknown fields rejected anywhere, ranges and enums), `Check` (known and unique factKeys, every `$` amount and percent present in or derived from the snapshot, no product, issuer, bank, ticker or promise of returns, length limits), `Service.Generate` (one call, one retry, then `fallback: true`; logs only hash, model, tokens and latency).
* `internal/recommendations/prompt/system.es.md`: versioned Spanish system prompt.
* `internal/recommendations/bedrock`: Converse adapter with a cache point after the system prompt and the answer forced through the `write_recommendations` tool. Temperature 0.2, 800 tokens.
* `ratelimit.go`: 10 requests per person per Bogotá day in Redis.
* `internal/app/app_test.go` (M0's file): no longer expects recommendations to be a stub.
* Golden tests on 5 fixture snapshots × 11 kinds of bad output, plus decode, HTTP, Bedrock adapter and settings tests.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test -race` pass for the module and for `internal/app` (run with Go 1.25 against the generated server, see problems).
* Not tested against the real Bedrock yet (manual staging run of the module prompt: 20 runs, same numbers).

##### Decisions and problems

* The response carries `modelId`, `promptVersion` and `snapshotHash` (RN-04 traceability).
* The snapshot guard runs as middleware before the generated server, because the generated types silently drop unknown fields.
* Settings come from the module's own environment variables (`RECOMMENDATIONS_MODEL_ID`, `RECOMMENDATIONS_AWS_REGION`, `RECOMMENDATIONS_DAILY_LIMIT`); M0 may prefer to move them into `platform/config` and `.env.example`.
* The build environment had only Go 1.24/1.25 and no access to proxy.golang.org. The code was compiled and tested with Go 1.25, the same oapi-codegen v2.8.1 and dependencies fetched from GitHub mirrors. CI with Go 1.27 is the real check.
* The app test change touches M0's file: needs Sofía's approval.

##### Next step

* Staging: set the Bedrock model access and run the 20-run manual check of Gate E.

#### 2026-10-28 · Scheduled reminders as local notifications (M12 step 2)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-70. Branch: feature/local-scheduled-notifications. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After #100 and #108 (expo-notifications) were merged, Karol asked what could go on while M2's vault is still open: "revisa en que podemos seguir". Step 2 of M12 no longer waited for anything, so Claude took it.

##### What was done

* `application/ports.ts`: new `LocalNotifier` port (permission, ask, schedule, cancel), and three store methods to keep the OS id of each reminder (`os_id` was already in the table).
* `application/notificationService.ts`:
  * `schedule` asks the phone for a local notification at the reminder's time. The first time, it asks permission after a short explanation. Without permission the reminder still reaches the inbox.
  * Scheduling the same id again replaces the OS notification, and `cancel` removes it.
  * Turning a kind off cancels its pending reminders.
  * A time already passed goes straight to `deliverDue`, and an OS failure never breaks `schedule`.
* `adapters/os/expoLocalNotifier.ts`: the port on expo-notifications, with a date trigger.
  * Lock screen privacy: an Android channel with `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE`, and an iOS category with `previewPlaceholder` ("Abre Fynk para ver el detalle.").
  * The explanation before the OS prompt is a native dialog ("Activa los recordatorios", "Ahora no", "Continuar").
  * Listeners: a fired reminder goes into the inbox, a tap opens its `deepLink`, including the tap that launched the app.
* `index.ts`: the service gets the notifier. The listeners start the first time the vault opens, so nothing native runs before there is an inbox (and Jest never touches the native module).
* Tests: 7 for the service with a fake notifier on the sql.js vault, and 11 for the adapter with expo-notifications mocked.

##### Result

* Tests: 55 suites and 738 tests of the app pass. ESLint, `tsc` and Prettier are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet.
* Not checked on a phone yet. The criterion's manual test is still to do: on a real Android and iPhone, a reminder fires with the app closed and the amount is hidden on the lock screen.

##### Decisions and problems

* The explanation is a native dialog, not a designed screen: the design has none. It is listed for design review.
* Android's private visibility hides the whole content on a secure lock screen, title included. That is stricter than "title only", and no amount ever shows.
* ESLint's `import/namespace` did not see the Android enums through `Notifications.*`, so they are imported by name.

##### Next step

Open the pull request. Then do the manual test on a phone once the vault and the lock screen of M2 are in `develop`. After that, `feature/notification-settings` (step 3).

#### 2026-10-28 · M13 Step 1: Append-only hash-chained audit store and verification

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-41. Branch: feature/audit-store-append-only. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Continue with the last assigned module (M13 Compliance, privacy and audit), starting with Phase 1: `feature/audit-store-append-only` (HU-027, SCRUM-41).

##### What was done

* Database Migrations (`services/api/internal/audit/migrations/`):
  * `1_create_events.up.sql`: Created `events` table in schema `audit` with fields `id`, `type`, `user_id`, `device_id`, `at`, `meta`, `prev_hash`, `hash`, and indexes on `user_id` and `at`.
  * Implemented `prevent_event_modification()` trigger function and triggers `trg_prevent_event_modification` (BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE) and `trg_prevent_event_truncate` (BEFORE TRUNCATE) enforcing database immutability.
  * Embedded migration files into `migrations.FS`.
* Sensitive Data Rejecter & Allow-list Validation (`services/api/internal/audit/validation.go`):
  * Strictly validated `ports.Event` against recognized event types (`ports.AuthLogin` through `ports.PrivacyDeletionCompleted`).
  * Enforced sensitive key detection rejecting forbidden terms (`amount`, `email`, `token`, `account_number`, `password`, `cedula`, `authorization`, `card`, `pan`, `cvv`, `jwt`, `secret`).
  * Validated `device_id` as valid UUID (when present) and metadata keys against type-specific allow-lists.
* Canonical Serialization & Hash Chaining (`services/api/internal/audit/canonical.go`):
  * Constructed deterministic canonical JSON encoding `CanonicalRow` with sorted map keys and microsecond-precision UTC timestamps (`2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z`).
  * Computed SHA-256 hash chaining: `hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical_json_of_row)` with a 32-byte zero slice for genesis row.
* PostgreSQL Audit Store (`services/api/internal/audit/store.go`):
  * Implemented `Store` implementing `ports.Recorder`.
  * `Record`: acquires PostgreSQL transaction advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`) to serialize append operations, fetches latest row hash as `prev_hash`, computes canonical hash, and inserts row.
  * `Verify`: scans rows ordered by `id ASC`, checks chain continuity (`prev_hash == previous_row.hash`), reconstructs canonical representation, and recalculates SHA-256 hashes to detect tampering or row removal.
* Verification Endpoint & CLI Command:
  * Added `GET /internal/audit/verify` handler in `services/api/internal/audit/http.go` and mounted on `app.NewRouter` with `auth.Require` and `app.WithAuditVerifier`.
  * Added CLI command `fynk-api audit verify` in `services/api/cmd/api/main.go`.
* Architectural Decision Record (`docs/adr/0004-audit-retention.md`):
  * Documented retention period proposal: 5 years for consent and privacy events (under Ley 1581 and statutory limitations for commercial claims) and 1 year for high-volume operational events.
* Tests:
  * Unit tests (`validation_test.go`, `canonical_test.go`, `http_test.go`, `app_test.go`).
  * Integration tests (`store_integration_test.go`) validating that `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, and `TRUNCATE` fail due to trigger, and verifying tamper/deletion detection.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passed with 0 issues.
* `make format-check` passed.
* `make test-go test-scripts` passed.

##### Decisions and problems

* Advisory transaction lock `pg_advisory_xact_lock(8472918471928471)` guarantees a strictly linear hash chain across concurrent API instances without table-level deadlocks.
* Truncating timestamps to microsecond precision before computing hashes avoids floating point/nanosecond discrepancies when reading back from PostgreSQL `timestamptz`.

#### 2026-11-01 · Cascade account deletion under Ley 1581 de 2012 (SCRUM-42)

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Antigravity. Jira: SCRUM-42. Branch: feature/account-deletion. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Implement the cascade account deletion endpoints for Module M13 per `docs/modules/M13-compliance.md` and Jira SCRUM-42 (HU-028):
1. `POST /privacy/deletion-requests`: requires authentication and fresh login in the last 5 minutes (`user.SessionID` verified via `identity.Sessions`). Executes cascade deletion in legal sequence:
   * Step 1: Revoke every consent through M4 (notifies bank provider).
   * Step 2: Mark every bank link revoked and delete bank link records.
   * Step 3: Delete backup records, recovery key, and device approvals.
   * Step 4: Delete registered devices.
   * Step 5: Delete Keycloak user via Keycloak admin API.
   * Step 6: Retain only deletion evidence (`privacy.deletion_requests`: request id, user id hash, timestamps, steps done) and immutable audit records.
2. `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}`: public endpoint returning completion status and legal confirmation message: `"Tu cuenta y tus datos fueron eliminados el <fecha>."`.
3. Unit and integration tests verifying zero rows remain for the deleted user across `identity`, `backup`, `bank`, and only revoked evidence in `consents`.

##### What was done

* **OpenAPI Contract (`contracts/modules/privacy.yaml`):**
  * Added `POST /privacy/deletion-requests` with optional `PrivacyDeletionRequestCreate` body (typed confirmation `ELIMINAR` and optional reason).
  * Added `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` with `security: []` (public) returning `PrivacyDeletionRequest`.
  * Updated schemas: `PrivacyDeletionRequest` (`id`, `status`, `createdAt`, `completedAt`, `stepsDone`, `message`) and `PrivacyDeletionRequestCreate`.
  * Ran `make generate` to update Go server interface and mobile TypeScript definitions.
* **Database Migration (`services/api/internal/privacy/migrations/`):**
  * Created `1_create_deletion_requests.up.sql` creating `privacy.deletion_requests` with `user_id_hash bytea NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id_hash) = 32)` and index on `user_id_hash`.
  * Created `1_create_deletion_requests.down.sql` dropping table and index.
  * Embedded migration files via `migrations.FS`.
* **Module Ports (`identity/ports` and `consents/ports`):**
  * `identity/ports`: added `UserDeleter` interface and `NewUserDeleter(cfg)` delegating to `kcadmin.Client.DeleteUser`.
  * `consents/ports`: added `ConsentsRevoker` interface and `NewConsentsRevoker(deps)` to safely revoke active and pending user consents with bank notification across module boundaries.
* **Privacy Module Implementation (`services/api/internal/privacy/`):**
  * `deleter.go`: implemented `Deleter` with `DeleteAccount` and `GetRequest`:
    * Fresh session verification against `identity.Sessions` within 5 minutes (returning `fresh_login_required` 403 on stale or missing session).
    * Confirmation validation (returning `invalid_confirmation` 400 if confirmation keyword mismatch).
    * Cascade deletion across consents, bank links, backup records, recovery keys, device approvals, identity devices, and Keycloak user.
    * Record audit events: `privacy.deletion_requested` and `privacy.deletion_completed`.
    * Record deletion evidence in `privacy.deletion_requests` storing only `sha256(user_id)` to preserve Zero-Knowledge privacy.
    * Formatted legal completion message in Spanish (`DeletionMessage`).
  * `server.go`: implemented `CreateDeletionRequest` (returning 202 Accepted) and `GetDeletionRequest` (returning 200 OK or 404).
  * `module.go`: wired `deleter`, returned `migrations.FS` in `Migrations()`, and allowed public access to `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` without authentication token.
* **Tests:**
  * Updated `services/api/internal/privacy/privacy_test.go` with unit tests for `POST /v1/privacy/deletion-requests` (unauthenticated, bad confirmation, success 202), public `GET /v1/privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` (404/200 without token), and fresh login verification (stale vs fresh session).
  * Added `deleter_integration_test.go` testing against PostgreSQL and asserting zero rows remain in `identity.devices`, `bank.links`, `backup.records`, `backup.recovery_keys`, and `backup.device_approvals`, while retaining revoked evidence in `consents.consents` and hashed evidence in `privacy.deletion_requests`.
  * Updated `services/api/internal/app/app_test.go` stub path list.

##### Result

* `make lint-go`: clean (0 issues).
* `make test-go test-scripts`: clean (100% passed).
* `make format-check`: clean.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Public status lookup after account deletion:** After an account is deleted, the user's Keycloak account and sessions are destroyed, meaning they cannot provide an Authorization token. Marked `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` with `security: []` in the OpenAPI specification and exempt in `requireAuth` middleware so users can check confirmation after account deletion.
* **Zero-Knowledge deletion evidence:** To comply with Colombian data protection regulations (Ley 1581) while respecting the Zero-Knowledge architecture, the deletion evidence table `privacy.deletion_requests` never stores cleartext `user_id`. Instead, it stores `sha256(user_id)` as 32-byte hash (`user_id_hash bytea NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id_hash) = 32)`).
* **Consents retention vs deletion:** Consents table rows are not deleted; they are marked `revoked` with timestamp and reason per legal requirement to prove compliance and consent lifecycle to authorities (Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio). All other user personal and financial records (`identity`, `bank`, `backup`) are deleted with zero remaining rows.

#### 2026-11-01 · M13 Step 2: Periodic Keycloak authentication events import job

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-41. Branch: feature/keycloak-events-import. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Continue with Module M13 (Compliance, privacy and audit), Phase 1: `feature/keycloak-events-import` (HU-027, SCRUM-41), implementing the periodic job that reads authentication events from Keycloak and records them into the append-only audit trail.

##### What was done

* Keycloak Configuration & Permissions:
  * `services/keycloak/realms/fynk-realm.json`: Added `view-events` role to `service-account-fynk-admin` clientRoles in `realm-management`, granting the confidential admin client permission to read the realm's event log.
  * `services/keycloak/README.md`: Updated `fynk-admin` client documentation to note `view-events` permission used for M13 authentication audit imports.
  * `services/api/internal/identity/keycloak/realm_test.go`: Updated `TestAdminClientIsAConfidentialServiceAccount` to verify `manage-users`, `view-events`, and `view-users`.
* Keycloak Admin Client Event Querying:
  * `services/api/internal/identity/kcadmin/client.go`: Added `AuthEvent` struct, `EventsFilter`, and `GetEvents(ctx, filter) ([]AuthEvent, error)` to query `/admin/realms/{realm}/events`.
  * `services/api/internal/identity/kcadmin/client_test.go`: Added unit test `TestGetEvents` testing query parameter formatting, authorization headers, and response decoding.
* Identity Module Port:
  * `services/api/internal/identity/ports/ports.go`: Exported `AuthEvent`, `AuthEvents` interface, and constructor `NewAuthEvents(cfg config.Config) AuthEvents`.
  * `services/api/internal/identity/ports/ports_test.go`: Added `TestAuthEventsRetrievesAndFiltersEvents`.
* Audit Database Migrations:
  * `services/api/internal/audit/migrations/2_create_keycloak_import.up.sql`: Created `audit.keycloak_events_cursor` for tracking the last synchronization timestamp, and `audit.keycloak_processed_events` for exactly-once event deduplication.
  * `services/api/internal/audit/migrations/2_create_keycloak_import.down.sql`: Drops created tables on rollback.
* Keycloak Event Importer Job (`services/api/internal/audit/importer.go`):
  * Implemented `KeycloakImporter` taking `*pgxpool.Pool`, `ports.Recorder`, `identityports.AuthEvents`, `*slog.Logger`, and `clock.Clock`.
  * `ImportOnce`:
    * Locks cursor row via `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` to serialize execution across multi-instance deployments.
    * Queries Keycloak events with a 5-minute safety buffer (`querySince = last_event_time - 5m`) to eliminate clock-skew and ingestion latency edge cases.
    * Filters for `LOGIN`, `LOGIN_ERROR`, `RESET_PASSWORD`, and `UPDATE_PASSWORD`.
    * Chronologically sorts events ascending before recording.
    * Deduplicates against `audit.keycloak_processed_events`.
    * Maps Keycloak events to `ports.AuthLogin`, `ports.AuthLoginFailed`, and `ports.AuthPasswordReset`.
    * Strictly purges sensitive fields: username, email addresses, and codes are never mapped to metadata. Only allow-listed attributes (`status`, `ip`, `auth_method`, `reason`, `error_code`) are persisted.
    * Records mapped events to the append-only cryptographic hash chain via `ports.Recorder`.
    * Prunes processed event records older than 7 days.
  * `Run`: Executes immediately and on a configurable ticker (`5 * time.Minute`), cleanly stopping when the context terminates.
* Wiring in Main API Server:
  * `services/api/cmd/api/main.go`: When `cfg.KeycloakAdminClientSecret` is configured, starts `KeycloakImporter.Run(ctx, 5*time.Minute)` in the background tied to the server shutdown context.
* Tests:
  * Unit tests (`importer_test.go`): Tests `mapKeycloakEvent` ensuring valid metadata, proper mapping of login, failure, and password reset events, and strict discarding of usernames/emails.
  * Integration tests (`store_integration_test.go`): Tests `KeycloakImporterIntegration` verifying end-to-end import, deduplication of already recorded events, cursor advancement, and valid audit hash chain integrity.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passed with 0 issues.
* `make test-go test-scripts` passed (all Go unit tests and scripts pass).
* `make format-check` passed.

##### Decisions and problems

* Keycloak's `GET /events` API date filtering operates on `yyyy-MM-dd` granularity. The importer passes the day boundary and performs microsecond-level timestamp filtering and exact-ID deduplication in PostgreSQL to prevent duplicate audit chain entries.
* A 5-minute query lookback window ensures no events are lost due to clock skew between container instances, while the `keycloak_processed_events` table ensures strict idempotency.

#### 2026-11-01 · Privacy policy and my data export (Ley 1581 de 2012)

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Antigravity. Jira: SCRUM-42, SCRUM-43. Branch: feature/privacy-policy-and-my-data. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Implement the Phase 4 compliance and privacy backend endpoints for Module M13 per `docs/modules/M13-compliance.md` and Jira SCRUM-42 (HU-028) & SCRUM-43 (HU-029):
1. `GET /privacy/policy`: public endpoint serving the active data treatment policy in Spanish (Markdown), detailing the data controller (Fynk Finance S.A.S.), authorized purposes (personal finance, informative recommendations, security/fraud prevention), data categories and storage locations following the Zero-Knowledge architecture in `CLAUDE.md`, data subject rights under Ley 1581 de 2012, contact channels, and response times (10 working days for queries, 15 working days for claims).
2. `GET /privacy/my-data`: authenticated endpoint exporting all cloud data stored for the authenticated user (Keycloak profile, registered devices, consents with audit evidence, bank links without token fingerprints, backup record count and size, and immutable audit events).

##### What was done

* **OpenAPI Contract (`contracts/modules/privacy.yaml`):**
  * Added `GET /privacy/policy` returning `PrivacyPolicy` with version `2026-11-v1`, SHA-256 integrity hash, response time guarantees, and full Spanish Markdown body.
  * Added `GET /privacy/my-data` returning `PrivacyDataExport` with schemas prefixed by `Privacy` per contract lint rules (`PrivacyProfileData`, `PrivacyDeviceData`, `PrivacyConsentData`, `PrivacyBankLinkData`, `PrivacyBackupData`, `PrivacyAuditEventData`).
  * Executed `make generate` to regenerate oapi-codegen Go server (`services/api/internal/privacy/http/api.gen.go`) and mobile TypeScript types (`apps/mobile/src/shared/api/schema.gen.ts`).
* **Identity Ports (`services/api/internal/identity/ports/ports.go`):**
  * Exported `UserProfile`, `UserProfileReader`, `NewUserProfileReader`, and `ErrUnavailable` to allow external modules (like `privacy`) to retrieve Keycloak profiles safely through module boundaries without violating `depguard`.
* **Privacy Module Implementation (`services/api/internal/privacy/`):**
  * `policy.go`: defined `CurrentPolicy()` with complete Colombian legal text under Ley 1581 de 2012 and Decreto 1377 de 2013, calculating SHA-256 checksum dynamically.
  * `collector.go`: implemented `Collector` reading user data from PostgreSQL schemas (`identity.devices`, `consents.consents`, `bank.links`, `backup.records`, `backup.recovery_keys`, and `audit.events`) while strictly suppressing any sensitive data or credential fingerprints (`token_fingerprint` omitted).
  * `server.go`: implemented `httpapi.StrictServerInterface` for `PrivacyHealth`, `GetPrivacyPolicy`, and `GetMyData`.
  * `module.go`: wired `New` and mounted routes under `requireAuth`, allowing public access to `/privacy/health` and `/privacy/policy` while protecting `/privacy/my-data`.
* **Tests:**
  * Added comprehensive unit tests in `services/api/internal/privacy/privacy_test.go` verifying policy content, legal requirements, SHA-256 integrity, data export aggregation, unauthenticated vs authenticated access, and stub route fallthrough.
  * Updated `services/api/internal/app/app_test.go` to test that `/v1/privacy/policy` is public and returns 200, while `/v1/privacy/deletion-requests` returns 501 `not_implemented`.

##### Result

* `make lint-go`: clean (0 issues).
* `make test-go test-scripts`: clean (100% passed).
* `make format-check`: all files formatted cleanly.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Depguard isolation:** Direct imports of `internal/identity/kcadmin` from `internal/privacy` were rejected by `depguard`. Created `UserProfileReader` interface and `NewUserProfileReader` constructor in `internal/identity/ports` so `privacy` accesses identity only through sanctioned port boundaries.
* **Sensitive credential suppression:** Per `M13-compliance.md` specification "bank links without fingerprints", the collector SQL query for `bank.links` explicitly selects only public metadata (`id, provider, consent_id, status, created_at, last_refreshed_at, revoked_at`) and excludes `token_fingerprint`.
* **Misspell linter on Spanish legal copy:** Marked the raw policy Markdown constant with `//nolint:misspell // Spanish copy complying with Ley 1581` to avoid false positives on legitimate Spanish legal vocabulary.

  * golangci-lint v2.15.0 passes on `./...`, and with `--build-tags integration` on `./internal/audit/...`. It runs locally through `go run`, as in the Makefile, so "not installed" was no reason to skip it.

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-46, SCRUM-48. Branch: docs/egress-cost-finding. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Juan Pablo Contreras requested to execute all steps of Module M14 (AWS Staging Infrastructure): "Please do the module m14 all the steps please."

##### What was done

* Created branch `docs/egress-cost-finding` based on `infra/deploy-pipeline`.
* Authored `docs/adr/0005-egress.md`:
  * Detailed architectural cost analysis comparing the confirmed design (7 Interface Endpoints in 1 AZ at ~$58.65/mo) against a single AWS NAT Gateway (~$36.95/mo).
  * Documented the trade-off finding: while a NAT Gateway saves ~$21.70/mo in staging, the confirmed interface endpoint design completely eliminates default outbound internet routes from private subnets, delivering zero-egress network isolation for financial data protection and compliance evidence.
  * Formally presented the recommendation to keep the confirmed design for staging and jury demo while logging the finding for team review.

##### Result

* ADR 0005 documented in `docs/adr/0005-egress.md`.
* Module M14 complete across all 7 planned steps.

##### Decisions and problems

* Respected the project rule: "Do not change confirmed architecture on your own; write findings into ADR 0005 and document for the team."

##### Next step

Module M14 is fully implemented across all 7 steps. Next module in backlog: M13 Compliance & Audit.

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-47. Branch: infra/monitoring-and-alerts. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Juan Pablo Contreras requested to execute all steps of Module M14 (AWS Staging Infrastructure): "Please do the module m14 all the steps please."

* `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/`:
  * Created SNS Topic `fynk-staging-alerts` for notifications with email subscription.
  * Configured CloudWatch Alarms per HU-033:
    1. ALB 5xx error rate > 5 in 5 minutes.
    2. Unhealthy target group hosts for 2 consecutive minutes.
    3. RDS CPU utilization > 80% for 10 minutes.
    4. RDS Free storage space < 2 GB.
    5. Caddy instance StatusCheckFailed >= 1.
    6. Custom metric `bank.sync_failed` > 10 in 15 minutes.
    7. Bedrock API invocation errors > 5 in 15 minutes.
  * Implemented PII leak detection metric filter (`fynk-staging-pii-leak-detector`) inspecting log events for unmasked bearer tokens, card numbers, or credentials.
  * Constructed CloudWatch Dashboard `Fynk-staging` tracking host availability, HTTP 2xx/5xx status rates, p95 response times across services, and bank sync outcomes.
* `docs/runbooks/incident.md`:
  * Incident classification (P1/P2/P3), diagnostic commands via AWS CLI/SSM, log inspection procedures, and Jira incident documentation workflow.

##### Result

* `terraform fmt -check` passed.
* `terraform validate` passed with configuration valid.

##### Decisions and problems

* Metric filter regexes were configured to actively flag accidental PII emissions in compliance with Colombian privacy laws (Ley 1581 / Circular Externa 004).

  * Security group allowing inbound HTTPS (443) and SMTP (465/587) from VPC CIDR.

Third of the three layers of step 4 of the simulated banks plan (store, oauth, api). Daniel's instruction was "continue" to step 4.

##### What was done

* `internal/api`: the bearer token is looked up in the registry, and only access tokens are accepted. `dev-token` and `test-token` still map to `consent-demo-001`. Any other token gets 401.
* A pending consent gets 403 `consent_not_authorized`. An account that is not covered by the consent gets 403 `account_not_in_consent`, and one that does not exist gets 404.
* `DELETE /open-finance/v1/consents/{id}` had no auth check: anyone who knew a consent id could revoke it. It now needs a client token (the Fynk API's revoke path), the consent's own access token, or a dev token for the demo consent. Otherwise it answers 401, or 403 for another consent's token.
* Tests in `internal/api/scoping_test.go`. Two older tests sent `Bearer consent-demo-001` after a revoke. That pass-through is gone, so they now send `dev-token` and still expect 403 `consent_revoked` (`internal/api/handlers_test.go`, `cmd/bank-simulator/mtls_test.go`).

##### Result

* `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` and `gofmt -l .` in `services/bank-simulator` pass on this commit alone. `fixtures/demo-user.json` is unchanged.
* Pull request: see the stack.

##### Decisions and problems

* Bug fixed: any bearer token that did not start with `consent-` read the demo user's consent, so every Fynk link showed the demo user's data.
* The `DELETE` check was added in review (Opus). The rest of the code is by a Sonnet subagent.
* Tokens and consents live in memory. After a simulator restart Fynk's tokens get 401 and the person links again, as before.
* A search for `consent-` bearer use found only the two simulator tests above. The Fynk API sends the access token from the token exchange, or a client token to revoke.

##### What was done

* `internal/oidc`: standard library OIDC client. Builds the code flow URL with PKCE S256, exchanges the code with `client_secret_post`, and checks the ID token: PS256 only, signature against the realm JWKS (cached, one refetch for an unknown kid), `iss`, `aud`/`azp`, `exp` and `nonce`. Tests use a fake Keycloak with a generated RSA key and cover RS256, HS256, `none`, a bad signature, wrong iss, aud and nonce, and an expired token.
* `internal/authorize`: `GET /oauth/authorize`, `GET /oauth/authorize/callback` and `POST /oauth/authorize/decision`. Server side sessions in memory with an opaque cookie (HttpOnly, SameSite Lax, Path `/oauth`, Secure when the request is TLS or `X-Forwarded-Proto` is https, 30 minutes). A login cookie ties the callback to the browser that started the login. The customer id is `fynk_sub`, else the demo id when `preferred_username` is the demo user, else the realm `sub`, checked with `demo.ValidCustomerRef`.
* Pages in Spanish (es-CO): the consent screen (entity, data, receiver Fynk, purpose, term in days) and "No tienes productos en <entidad>." with a "Volver a Fynk" button. Every page has the "Banco simulado para pruebas. No es un banco real." banner.
* `internal/portal/style.go`: the portal CSS and banner moved to constants so the new pages reuse them. `domain.EntityDisplayName`, `demo.ValidCustomerRef`, `oauth.Service.LookupPAR` and `DropPAR` were added.
* `cmd/bank-simulator`: new config `BANK_PUBLIC_URL`, `BANK_OIDC_ISSUER`, `BANK_OIDC_BACKCHANNEL_URL`, `BANK_OIDC_CLIENT_ID`, `BANK_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET`. `newBankMux` takes an optional authorize config. With no issuer or no secret the page answers 503.
* `contracts/bank-simulator.yaml`: added the callback and decision paths and the 503 answer.
* `cmd/bank-simulator/authorize_test.go`: 12 tests. They cover 503 when not configured, unknown request_uri and client_id mismatch (400), a Fynk user authorizing and the token reading only that entity, no products (message, no active consent, a forced authorize still fails), cancel, a second entity with an existing session, the bank's own user, the demo user, a missing or wrong CSRF token (403), a forged callback, cookie flags and that logs hold no sub, email, code, secret or token.

* The page uses 302 to send the person to the realm and 303 after a POST.
* `CompleteAuthorization` builds its redirect without escaping, so the page builds its own redirect from the returned code and the PAR state.

The OIDC client, the helpers and the contract described above are in the two PRs below this one. #### 2026-11-06 · Banco Demo: PAR per entity and opaque tokens

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-73. Branch: feature/simulated-banks-entity-oauth. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second of the three layers of step 4 of the simulated banks plan (store, oauth, api). Daniel's instruction was "continue" to step 4.

##### What was done

* `internal/oauth`: PAR requires `entity`, one of the four provider ids. Otherwise it answers 400 `invalid_request`. It keeps the optional `nonce` and `fynk_consent_id` and creates a pending consent with no customer.
* `CompleteAuthorization` activates the consent through `store.ActivateConsent`. A customer with no products at the entity gets `ErrNoProductsAtEntity`, and the consent stays pending.
* Tokens are opaque (`at-`, `rt-` and `cc-` plus 32 hex) and registered in the store with their consent and client. The consent id is no longer part of the token.
* Refresh looks up the refresh token. An unknown or expired token, or one whose consent is no longer active, gets `invalid_grant`.
* Revoke looks up the token. An unknown token is a 200 that revokes nothing (RFC 7009), with no fallback to the demo consent.
* Tests in `internal/oauth/entity_test.go`. In `oauth_test.go`, the PAR calls now send `entity=banco-demo` and the revoke step sends the access token instead of the consent id.

##### Result

* `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` and `gofmt -l .` in `services/bank-simulator` pass on this commit alone.
* Pull request: see the stack.

##### Decisions and problems

Bugs fixed:

* PAR bound every consent to the demo customer and made it active at once.
* Tokens looked like `at-consent-<hex>-tok-<hex>`. Revoke and refresh split them on `-` and took `consent` as the consent id, so they acted on the wrong consent.

Not yet fixed: the Open Finance API still reads any bearer token as the demo consent. The api slice above this one fixes that. The Fynk API adapter does not send `entity` yet, so its integration test against a running simulator fails at PAR until step 6.

##### Next step

The api slice: look tokens up on every Open Finance call.

#### 2026-11-06 · Ingresar con Fynk in the realms and the compose stack

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-74. Branch: feature/simulated-banks-fynk-login. PR: none. Status: partial.

##### Instruction

Daniel said "continue with the rest of the steps". This session is step 5 of the simulated banks plan, slice 5b: the realm changes and the compose wiring that the authorization page of slice 5a needs (HU-057).

##### What was done

* `services/keycloak/realms/fynk-realm.json` (M1): confidential client `banco-demo-broker`, standard flow only, PKCE S256, secret `${BANCO_DEMO_BROKER_SECRET}`, redirect `http://localhost:8081/realms/banco-demo/broker/fynk/endpoint`. Its only default scope is `basic` (the `sub`) and it has one ID token mapper that repeats the user id as the claim `fynk_sub`. No email or name scope.
* `services/keycloak/realms/banco-demo-realm.json` (M16): identity provider `fynk` ("Ingresar con Fynk", authorization URL on `localhost:8081`, token and JWKS URLs on `keycloak:8080`, PKCE, signature validation with the JWKS, no user info call, secret `${BANCO_DEMO_BROKER_SECRET}`). First broker login uses the new flow `fynk first broker login` (create user if unique, auto link, no review-profile). Mappers: an attribute importer for `fynk_sub` and a username template `${ALIAS}.${CLAIM.fynk_sub}`. `bank-portal` got `client-secret` with `${BANK_PORTAL_CLIENT_SECRET}`, PKCE S256, no default scopes, and ID token mappers for `sub`, `preferred_username` and `fynk_sub`.
* The same realm also got a PS256 key provider (the simulator accepts PS256 only and the realm had no PS256 key) and its own user profile with an optional email. `registrationEmailAsUsername` is now `false`, because a brokered user has no email.
* `docker-compose.yml` and `.env.example` (M0 files): the two secrets for Keycloak and the `BANK_*` OIDC variables for the simulator, fake values ending in `-local-only`.
* `services/keycloak/README.md`: the new client, a section on the broker, the two secrets, and a note that existing local volumes need a re-import.
* Tests in `services/api/internal/identity/keycloak/`: one in `realm_test.go` for the fynk client, and `bancodemo_realm_test.go` for the banco-demo realm (provider and label, no review-profile flow, no email dependency, `fynk_sub` importer and ID token mapper, `bank-portal` client, PS256 key, no literal secret). The integration test environment got the two new secrets.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./internal/identity/keycloak/...` passes, golangci-lint v2.15.0 with the `integration` tag reports 0 issues on that package, `docker compose config` is valid with `.env.example`.
* Not verified: the Docker daemon was not running on this machine, so Keycloak was never started with these files. The realm JSON follows Keycloak 26 behavior from memory and the static tests only check the file, not the import. The brokered login, the `fynk_sub` claim and the "Ingresar con Fynk" button on the login page still need a real run.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* The `fynk_sub` claim repeats the `sub` under another name. I was not sure the attribute importer and the username template can read the standard `sub` claim, and a claim with another name always works. It carries no extra data.
* An empty `defaultClientScopes` plus explicit mappers on `bank-portal`, because the realm declares its own `clientScopes` and the built-in ones may not exist there.
* The realm URLs are fixed to `localhost:8081` and `keycloak:8080`. Staging (M14) will need its own values and the two new secrets in Secrets Manager.
* The identity provider cannot pin PS256 itself: Keycloak checks the signature with the algorithm in the token header. The simulator is the one that rejects every algorithm but PS256.

##### Next step

Start Docker, reset the local Keycloak volume, and check the login page, a brokered login and the ID token. Then M14 adds the secrets and staging URLs.

#### 2026-11-06 · Simulator generator: 12 months of history built backwards

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-73. Branch: feature/simulated-banks-history. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Step 3 of the approved plan, slice B: replace the opening-only transactions of non-demo customers with 12 months of history, built backwards from the final balances, keeping the demo user untouched.

##### What was done

* `services/bank-simulator/internal/domain/history.go`: monthly fixed items (salary, rent, utilities, 4 subscriptions, transport, gym, card payment on day 5), variable spending scaled to the salary, transfers to the other entities (wallet, Banco Digital, Cooperativa APORTE and interest), the CDT funding, and the opening balance computed as final balance minus movements.
* A fixer keeps savings and wallet accounts from going below zero: it adds extra purchases after the lowest point, which raises the earlier balances and leaves the final one and the fixed items alone.
* Cards: charges stay under 35 % of the limit each month, and a last payment brings each card down to its final debt.
* `opening.go`: transactions are now sorted so that reading them backwards gives the same order the history was built in. The opening-only builder was removed. `entities.go` calls the history builder, and the Banco Demo card always has due day 5.
* `history_test.go`: determinism; over 1.000 customers, balances and limits along the whole history, sums, subscriptions in the last 12 complete months, salary and rent, transport on the card without a wallet, both sides of own transfers, provider ids, order and unique ids.

##### Result

* Tests: all 34 tests of the simulator pass, none fail. The domain package takes about 6.5 seconds, of which the history tests take about 4. `go vet` and golangci-lint (Makefile version) report 0 issues.
* `git diff --exit-code fixtures/demo-user.json` is clean after `go test ./...`.
* `gofmt -l .` still lists `internal/store/store.go`, which was already unformatted before this work and was not touched.
* Pull request: none, not pushed.

##### Decisions and problems

* Transactions dated after the reference time on the current day are dropped, so no movement is in the future.
* The current month is partial, so its variable spending is scaled by the days elapsed.
* The CDT account gets a credit on its opening date, so its transactions add up to its balance.
* Cooperativa APORTE is 3 % of its final balance per month, so its opening balance stays positive.

##### Review (same session)

* The first fixer added each extra purchase right after the lowest point. That left the balance just as low, so it looped and piled up to 48 purchases on one account in one day. It now adds them after the movement that ends the last negative stretch, spread up to the reference time, in one pass. Over 1.000 customers it runs 58 times, with at most 5 groceries or shopping purchases on one account in one day.
* New `TestHistoryAtOtherDates`: the same balance and limit rules at six more reference dates (around day 5, month ends, a leap day and the new year), with no movement in the future.
* Banco Demo Ahorros starts the window $2M to $7M above its final balance. That is expected: the 13-month window ends on the 27th, before payday, so it holds 12 salaries and almost 13 months of spending.

##### Next step

Use `EntitySummaries` in `POST /demo/v1/customers`.

#### 2026-11-06 · Banco Demo: create a demo customer from the Fynk sub over mTLS

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-73. Branch: feature/simulated-banks-provisioning. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel said "continue" to step 4 of the approved simulated banks plan. This is the second slice: the demo-only endpoint that creates the customer at sign up. It sits on top of the branch `feature/simulated-banks-api-scoping`.

##### What was done

* `internal/demo`: `POST /demo/v1/customers`. It answers 404 problem+json without a client certificate, 401 without a registered unexpired client token, 403 without the `demo:provision` scope, and 400 for a bad `customerRef` or bad JSON (body limit 4 KB, pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$`). It returns the entities from `EntitySummaries` and logs nothing.
* `internal/oauth`: the `client_credentials` grant now requires a client assertion (401 `invalid_client`), grants only the scope asked for, and gives `demo:provision` only to the client set with `WithProvisionClientID` (otherwise 400 `invalid_scope`). With no scope it still returns the admin token that the Fynk API uses to revoke a consent. The token is registered as kind client.
* `cmd/bank-simulator`: `config.ProvisionClientID` from `BANK_PROVISION_CLIENT_ID`, default `fynk`. `newBankMux` takes it, and the new route is registered there.
* Tests in `cmd/bank-simulator/provision_test.go`: idempotent answer, different customers, the demo customer, every access rule, bad bodies, scope rules for `client_credentials`, and a log-capture test that the customerRef and the token never appear in the logs.

##### Result

* Tests: 56 test and subtest passes, 0 failures, from `go test -v ./...` in `services/bank-simulator`. `go vet` and golangci-lint v2.15.0 report nothing. `gofmt -l .` prints nothing. `fixtures/demo-user.json` is unchanged.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* The demo customer id is an email, so the `customerRef` pattern would reject it. The handler accepts that one constant as an exception, so the endpoint can return the demo user's two entities. Any other email gets 400.
* A scope other than `demo:provision` in `client_credentials` gets `invalid_scope`.
* The handler does not need the provision client id, because the scope on the token already carries that check. Only the oauth service takes it.
* The route sits on the mux that both listeners share. It protects itself by answering 404 when the request has no client certificate. Splitting the muxes per listener is not done in this slice.
* Tokens and consents live in memory. After a simulator restart Fynk's tokens get 401 and the person has to link again. That is the same as before this work.

##### Next step

Step 5: the authorization page, which calls `CompleteAuthorization`, and "Ingresar con Fynk" in both realms.

#### 2026-11-07 · Banco Demo: history timing test fails on CI

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-73. Branch: fix/simulated-banks-history-timing-test. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel said "some of the PRs fail the CI".

##### What was done

* Every failing PR of stacks #314 and #323 and `develop` itself (since de81f3d) failed the same test: `TestHistoryRunsFast` in `services/bank-simulator/internal/domain`, at 20.1 to 20.3 s against its 20 s budget.
* CI runs `make test-go GOTESTFLAGS="-race -covermode=atomic -coverprofile=coverage.out"`. The race detector makes the generator about ten times slower (the package took about 123 s on CI and the test takes about 1 s locally), so the budget only holds by chance there.
* `race_on_test.go` and `race_off_test.go` set `raceEnabled` by build tag, and the test skips itself when it is true. Without `-race` the 20 s budget still applies. The other tests of the package still run on CI.

##### Result

* Locally: `go vet ./...`, `go test` (the test passes in about 1 s) and golangci-lint v2.15.0 with the `integration` tag pass. `-race` cannot run on this PC (32-bit Windows Go toolchain), so the skip under `-race` is checked by CI.
* Pull request: see the PR of this branch.

##### Decisions and problems

* Skipping under `-race` was chosen over raising the budget, because any fixed budget under the race detector depends on the runner's speed.
* The test came from the generator work (#307/#308), which was already merged, so `develop` was red.

##### Next step

After this merges, rebase stacks #314 and #323 on `develop` so their CI runs green.

#### 2026-10-28 · Use GitHub's stacked pull requests for dependent work

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: docs/stacked-prs-with-gh-stack. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While M1 was being finished, Daniel asked: "why dont you do the stacks with this https://docs.github.com/en/pull-requests/how-tos/stacked-pull-requests". After the M1 stacks merged, he asked to "tell to claude.md to do the stack of PRs with the github stack tool".

##### What was done

* **In practice:** M1's dependent pull requests became two GitHub stacks with the `gh stack` extension.
  * #81: #74 → #76 → #75 → #79 → #83 → #84.
  * #82: #77 → #78.

  Both merged bottom-up into `develop`.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Pull requests:** a rule that dependent work goes in a stack, with how a stack is targeted, reviewed and merged, and its limits.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Steps Claude Code follows:** the `gh stack` commands to start, rebase, submit and link a stack. The never-list now names `gh stack merge` next to `gh pr merge`.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Decisions taken:** a "Stacked pull requests (28 Oct)" row.
* **`.claude/settings.json`:** `gh stack merge` is denied like `gh pr merge`, so Claude Code still can't merge.

##### Result

* `make format-check` passes.
* With the stacks, the PRs above were rebased and retargeted by GitHub after each merge. Before that, "depends on #N" pull requests on `develop` needed a manual rebase, and one failed CI because it lacked a fix from the pull request below it.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No reordering of independent work into one stack.** A stack is linear, and a pull request cannot merge before the ones under it.
* **This changes shared files owned by M0** (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/settings.json`), so Sofia reviews it.

##### Next step

Every module uses `gh stack` for its dependent pull requests.

* F1 "al menos un caracter" was read as "at least one special character"; to confirm.
* Root causes found while mapping: V7 Billetera passes the fixed "Invitado" name to the header; V12 `SegmentedControl` is built for the blue header (white text) and is used on the page background; F3 the Dashboard has a `TODO(M5)` for pull to refresh; V2 the Dashboard never renders M8's goals.
* The Jira project has no Bug type, so bugs were filed as Tarea with priority High (V2, V12, F2, F5).

##### Next step

Confirm the F1 reading, then start with the High priority tasks: F2 (onboarding not shown), F5 (cannot add money), V12 (unreadable selectors) and V2 (goals missing on the Dashboard).
